## Implementation Tasks

### Task 1: GEMM — `get_torch_quant` JIT bypass (target: match ref ~24 us, then optimize)

**Objective:** Replace `ref_kernel` delegation with our own quantize-then-GEMM pipeline using
`get_torch_quant` (pure PyTorch, no Triton JIT) to avoid the call-site correctness bug.

**Key insight:** MoE `reference.py:109` already calls `get_torch_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)`
successfully. The GEMM `reference.py:95` uses `get_triton_quant` which has the call-site bug.
If `get_torch_quant` with `shuffle=True` produces compatible output for `gemm_a4w4`, we bypass
the JIT issue entirely.

**Files:** `kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py`

**Implementation (attempt 1 — torch_quant with shuffle):**
```python
import aiter
from aiter import QuantType, dtypes
from task import input_t, output_t

def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
    A = A.contiguous()
    quant_func = aiter.get_torch_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
    A_q, A_scale = quant_func(A, shuffle=True)
    return aiter.gemm_a4w4(A_q, B_shuffle, A_scale, B_scale_sh,
                           dtype=dtypes.bf16, bpreshuffle=True)
```

**Fallback (attempt 2 — manual shuffle):**
```python
from aiter.ops.shuffle import shuffle_weight
from aiter.utility import fp4_utils

quant_func = aiter.get_torch_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
A_q, A_scale = quant_func(A)  # no shuffle kwarg
A_q_shuffled = shuffle_weight(A_q.view(m, k // 2), layout=(16, 16))
A_scale_shuffled = fp4_utils.e8m0_shuffle(A_scale)
```

**Fallback (attempt 3 — hip_quant):**
```python
quant_func = aiter.get_hip_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
A_q, A_scale = quant_func(A, shuffle=True)
```

**Verify:** `--mode test` → 0.0 max error → `--mode benchmark` → `--mode leaderboard`

```python
    return gemm_afp4wfp4(A_q, B_q, A_scale, B_scale, ...)
```

### Task 3: MoE — Remote aiter introspection (0 risk, high info value)

**Objective:** Submit a test script that prints aiter internal state to understand
what CK kernel configs, tile sizes, and CSV tuning data are available. This uses
Popcorn's stderr output to dump diagnostic information.

**Files:** `kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission.py`

**Implementation:**
```python
import sys, os
from task import input_t, output_t
from aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe, get_2stage_cfgs
from aiter import ActivationType, QuantType

def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    (hidden_states, gate_up_weight, down_weight,
     gate_up_weight_scale, down_weight_scale,
     gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,
     gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled, down_weight_scale_shuffled,
     topk_weights, topk_ids, config) = data

    # Dump aiter CK kernel selection info to stderr
    M = hidden_states.shape[0]
    E = gate_up_weight_shuffled.shape[0]
    print(f"INTROSPECT: M={M}, E={E}, config={config}", file=sys.stderr)

    # Try to read tuned CSV configs
    import aiter
    aiter_path = os.path.dirname(aiter.__file__)
    csv_path = os.path.join(aiter_path, "configs")
    if os.path.isdir(csv_path):
        for f in sorted(os.listdir(csv_path)):
            fpath = os.path.join(csv_path, f)
            if f.endswith('.csv'):
                print(f"CSV: {f}", file=sys.stderr)
                with open(fpath) as fh:
                    for i, line in enumerate(fh):
                        if i < 5:  # first 5 lines (header + samples)
                            print(f"  {line.rstrip()}", file=sys.stderr)

    # Still return correct output (delegate to reference)
    from reference import ref_kernel
    return ref_kernel(data)
```

**Verify:** `--mode test` → read STDERR output for CSV configs and shape info.
No risk to correctness since we delegate to `ref_kernel`.

### Task 5: MLA — Try MXFP4 KV cache (input already provides it)

**Objective:** The input `kv_data` dict provides three KV cache formats:
- `"bf16"`: Tensor (total_kv, 1, 576) — highest precision
- `"fp8"`: (Tensor, Tensor) — current reference uses this
- `"mxfp4"`: (Tensor, Tensor) — fp4x2 (total_kv, 1, 288) + scale

We've only tested fp8. MXFP4 has 2x less memory bandwidth (4-bit vs 8-bit),
which could significantly speed up the memory-bound decode kernel. The question
is whether `mla_decode_fwd` supports MXFP4 KV, or if we need a different kernel path.

**Files:** `kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py`

**Implementation:**
```python
# Test if mla_decode_fwd accepts fp4x2 dtype KV buffer
kv_buffer_mxfp4, kv_scale_mxfp4 = kv_data["mxfp4"]
# Need to check: does mla_decode_fwd support fp4x2 dtype?
# If not, we need aiter's MXFP4-specific MLA kernel (if it exists)
```

**Discovery step:** Submit a version that prints `type(kv_buffer_mxfp4.dtype)` and
tries to call `mla_decode_fwd` with it. The error message will reveal whether MXFP4
is supported.

### Task 6: Deep source reading — aiter tuned CSV dump

**Objective:** Extract the full auto-tuning CSV configurations from the remote MI355X
environment. These CSVs drive the CK kernel selection in `fused_moe` and potentially
`gemm_a4w4`. Understanding them reveals what tile configs exist for each shape.

**Files:** `kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py` (any kernel will do)

**Implementation:** Same pattern as Task 3 — submit a script that reads and prints
the aiter configs directory, then delegates to ref_kernel.

```python
import os, sys, aiter
from reference import ref_kernel
from task import input_t, output_t

def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    aiter_path = os.path.dirname(aiter.__file__)
    # Print all CSV content (not just headers)
    for root, dirs, files in os.walk(os.path.join(aiter_path, "configs")):
        for f in sorted(files):
            if f.endswith('.csv'):
                fpath = os.path.join(root, f)
                print(f"\n=== {f} ===", file=sys.stderr)
                with open(fpath) as fh:
                    print(fh.read(), file=sys.stderr)
    return ref_kernel(data)
```

**Verify:** `--mode test` → read STDERR for full CSV contents

# Popcorn CLI AMD Kernel Submission Workflow

## Submission Commands

```bash
CLI=~/.local/bin/popcorn-cli
KERNELS=/path/to/kernels/dir

# 1. Test (correctness check — do this first)
$CLI submit --no-tui --mode test --gpu MI355X --leaderboard <name> $KERNELS/<kernel>/submission.py

# 2. Benchmark (timing, no leaderboard impact)
$CLI submit --no-tui --mode benchmark --gpu MI355X --leaderboard <name> $KERNELS/<kernel>/submission.py

# 3. Leaderboard (official ranked submission)
$CLI submit --no-tui --mode leaderboard --gpu MI355X --leaderboard <name> $KERNELS/<kernel>/submission.py
```

## Leaderboard Name Pattern

Leaderboard names follow the pattern: `amd-<directory-name>`

| Kernel directory | Leaderboard name |
|-----------------|-----------------|
| `mxfp4-mm/`    | `amd-mxfp4-mm`  |
| `mixed-mla/`   | `amd-mixed-mla` |
| `moe-mxfp4/`   | `amd-moe-mxfp4` |

To discover unknown names: submit with `--mode test` using candidate names. A 404/does-not-exist
response means the name is wrong; acceptance (even with correctness failures) confirms the name.

## Submission Limits (Luma AMD Speedrun)

- **No per-user rate limit** — submit as many times as needed
- Server-wide: 3 concurrent submissions (asyncio.Semaphore)
- ~10 requests/second global rate limit
- Per-mode timeouts: test=180s, benchmark=180s (overridden per competition in task.yml)
- **Total workflow timeout: ~12 minutes** — the entire GitHub Actions run must complete within this
- Use `--mode test` first, then `--mode benchmark`, then `--mode leaderboard`

## aiter JIT Compilation Times (Luma AMD Speedrun)

aiter builds JIT modules on first use. These builds happen **inside** the 12-minute workflow timeout:

| JIT Module | Build Time |
|-----------|-----------|
| `module_moe_sorting` | ~25s |
| `module_moe_ck2stages_fp4x2_*` | ~103s |
| `module_mla_metadata` | ~32s |
| `module_mla_asm` | ~21s |
| `module_mla_reduce` | ~171s |

**Critical implications:**
- MoE JIT builds alone take ~230s (~4 min), leaving only ~8 min for actual execution
- MLA JIT builds take ~224s total — similar headroom concern
- When the server queue is backed up with multiple concurrent submissions, the JIT time can push the total over the 12-minute limit
- Submissions that appear to fail silently (no output, cancelled status) are often **queue pressure timeouts**, not code errors

**Mitigation:** Retry submissions during off-peak hours. JIT results are cached between runs in the same workflow, so a retry that hits a pre-warmed runner will be much faster.

## Local Testing Limitations on gfx1151 (RDNA4 Strix Halo)

Local GPU testing on gfx1151 (AMD Radeon 8060S, Strix Halo iGPU) is NOT feasible with ROCm 6.2.4:

- Without override: `HIP error: invalid device function` (ISA mismatch)
- With `HSA_OVERRIDE_GFX_VERSION=11.0.0`: "Memory access fault by GPU node-2" on kernel launch
  (RDNA4 gfx1151 is not binary-compatible with RDNA3 gfx1100)
- ROCm 6.3+ would be needed for gfx1151 native support

**Workaround**: All testing via Popcorn CLI remote submission (MI355X hardware).

## `uv run` Reverting ROCm Torch Install

The project's uv lockfile pins CUDA torch (`torch+cu128`). Running `uv run` syncs from the
lockfile and overwrites any manually installed ROCm torch.

**Fix**: Create an isolated venv outside the project for GPU kernel work:

```bash
# Create isolated ROCm venv
python3 -m venv research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/.venv-rocm
source research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/.venv-rocm/bin/activate
pip install torch --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/rocm6.2.4

# Use this venv directly (not uv run) for kernel testing
python submission.py
```

## Competition Kernel Structure

Each kernel must implement:
```python
# submission.py
from task import input_t, output_t

def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    ...  # Your implementation
```

The eval harness (`eval.py`) calls `generate_input()` from `reference.py`, passes `_clone_data(data)`
to `custom_kernel`, then runs `check_implementation()` comparing output to reference. Benchmark
runs clear L2 cache between runs (`clear_l2_cache_large()`), so cache warming tricks don't help.

## Troubleshooting: Transient Artifact Download Failures

Popcorn CLI submissions run on GitHub Actions runners. The evaluation job and the artifact
download are separate steps — a network failure can cause the CLI to report failure even though
the evaluation job itself **succeeded**.

**Symptoms:**
- CLI output shows failure / no results despite a correct kernel
- Error mentions artifact download or GitHub Actions artifact retrieval

**Fix:** Simply retry the same submission command. A retry succeeds in the majority of cases.

```bash
# Retry exactly the same command — the eval job is idempotent
$CLI submit --no-tui --mode benchmark --gpu MI355X --leaderboard <name> <file>
```

**Important:** Transient failures look identical to actual correctness failures in the CLI output.
If a previously-passing kernel suddenly "fails", try a retry before debugging the kernel code.

## Correctness Tolerances (Luma AMD Speedrun)

| Kernel | rtol | atol |
|--------|------|------|
| MXFP4 GEMM | 1e-2 | 1e-2 |
| MLA Decode | 1e-2 | 1e-2 |
| MoE MXFP4 | 5e-2 | 5e-2 |

# aiter Kernel Parameter Semantics (AMD MI355X)

## Problem

aiter's `fused_moe` and `mla_decode_fwd` have parameters that sound like performance flags
but actually change computation or have counter-intuitive effects on MI355X.

## fused_moe: doweight_stage1

### What it does

`doweight_stage1=True` moves the `topk_weights` multiplication from **stage 2** (after SiLU
activation) to **stage 1** (before SiLU activation).

- `doweight_stage1=False` (default): `output = SiLU(gate(x)) * up(x)`, then multiply weights in stage 2
- `doweight_stage1=True`: multiply weights in stage 1 BEFORE activation

### Why it causes correctness failure

SiLU is a **nonlinear** activation. Multiplying weights before vs after a nonlinear activation
produces mathematically different results:

```
w * SiLU(gate(x)) * up(x)   ≠   SiLU(w * gate(x)) * (w * up(x))
```

This is not a subtle numerical difference — it produces ~80% element mismatches with 10-30%
per-element error (well above the 5e-2 tolerance for MoE).

**Rule:** `doweight_stage1=True` only produces correct results for linear activations (ReLU-like),
not for SiLU/GELU. For MXFP4 MoE with SiLU, always use `doweight_stage1=False`.

## fused_moe: OPUS Sorting (AITER_USE_OPUS_MOE_SORTING)

Setting `os.environ["AITER_USE_OPUS_MOE_SORTING"] = "1"` before calling `fused_moe` switches
to the OPUS sorting backend (`moe_sorting_opus_fwd` instead of `moe_sorting_fwd`).

**Correctness:** OPUS sorting only changes token dispatch order — the computation is identical.
Setting this env var passes correctness tests (verified, max error 0.015625).

**Performance on MXFP4 variant:** NO EFFECT. The env var is not wired into the fp4x2 code path
in `fused_moe.py`. JIT still builds `module_moe_sorting` (not `module_moe_sorting_opus`), and
benchmark times are identical to reference. This was verified by running the full benchmark suite.

## fused_moe: block_size_M

Override the auto-selected tile size for the token dimension:

```python
fused_moe(..., block_size_M=64)  # or 32, 128
```

Default is `-1` (auto-selects from tuned CSV configs). For MXFP4 with different model configs:
- bs=8 (small): auto-selects `block_m=32`
- bs=128 (medium): auto-selects `block_m=64`

**DANGER: Forcing `block_size_M=64` causes GPU memory access faults on benchmark configs.**
When `estimated_m_per_expert` is low (e.g., bs=16 with 256 experts = ~1 token/expert), a tile
size of 64 exceeds the actual data bounds and causes a hardware-level `Memory access fault by
GPU node-2`. The auto-tuner correctly selects smaller tiles for these shapes. **Never override
`block_size_M` without verifying it's safe for ALL shapes in the benchmark suite.**

## mla_decode_fwd: fast_mode is SLOWER on MI355X

### What it does

`fast_mode=True` in `get_mla_metadata_info_v1` and `get_mla_metadata_v1` changes the work
distribution strategy: `(bs + cu_num) * tiles` work items vs `tile_cnt * cu_num`.

### Test results (MI355X, DeepSeek R1 MLA, fp8)

| Config | fast_mode=False (reference) | fast_mode=True, adaptive splits | fast_mode=True, fixed splits=32 |
|--------|----------------------------|--------------------------------|--------------------------------|
| bs=4, kv=1024 | ~131 us | ~154 us | ~152 us |
| bs=256, kv=8192 | ~462 us | ~536 us | ~542 us |
| Geomean | ~191 us | ~224 us (+17%) | ~232 us (+21%) |

**fast_mode=True is 17-21% SLOWER than fast_mode=False on MI355X for decode workloads.**

The reference implementation already uses well-tuned defaults. Changing `fast_mode` to `True`
and `num_kv_splits` from 32 to adaptive values (4-16 depending on batch size) both made
performance worse, not better.

**Rule:** For MLA decode on MI355X with the DeepSeek R1 forward_absorb architecture, use:
```python
fast_mode=False
num_kv_splits=32
intra_batch_mode=True
```

## fused_moe: Parameter Quick Reference

| Parameter | Default | Effect | Safe to Change? |
|-----------|---------|--------|----------------|
| `doweight_stage1` | `False` | **Changes computation** — must be `False` for SiLU | NO (SiLU fails) |
| `block_size_M` | `-1` (auto) | Changes CK tile size | **DANGEROUS** — GPU memory fault if too large for shape |
| `moe_sorting_dispatch_policy` | `0` | Token dispatch strategy | Yes but 20-79% SLOWER at policy=1 |
| `AITER_USE_OPUS_MOE_SORTING` | unset | Dispatch backend | No effect on MXFP4 variant |

## mla_decode_fwd: Parameter Quick Reference

| Parameter | Reference Value | Effect | Change? |
|-----------|----------------|--------|---------|
| `fast_mode` | `False` | Work distribution strategy | NO — slower on MI355X |
| `num_kv_splits` | `32` | Split count for KV attention | Tested 4-16, all slower |
| `intra_batch_mode` | `True` | Intra-batch parallelism | Do not change |
| `page_size` | `1` | KV page size | Do not change |

## Context

Discovered during Luma AMD Speedrun competition (March 2026) on AMD MI355X.
Tests run via Popcorn CLI remote submission. ~240s JIT build time means only ~8 min
remains in the 12-min workflow for actual parameter sweep testing.

# Executing Plans

## Overview

Load plan, review critically, execute tasks in batches, report for review between batches.

**Core principle:** Batch execution with checkpoints for architect review.

**Announce at start:** "I'm using the executing-plans skill to implement this plan."

## The Process

### Step 1: Load and Review Plan
1. Read plan file
2. Review critically - identify any questions or concerns about the plan
3. If concerns: Raise them with your human partner before starting
4. If no concerns: Create TodoWrite and proceed

### Step 2: Execute Batch
**Default: First 3 tasks**

For each task:
1. Mark as in_progress
2. Follow each step exactly (plan has bite-sized steps)
3. Run verifications as specified
4. Mark as completed

### Step 3: Report
When batch complete:
- Show what was implemented
- Show verification output
- Say: "Ready for feedback."

### Step 4: Continue
Based on feedback:
- Apply changes if needed
- Execute next batch
- Repeat until complete

### Step 5: Complete Development

After all tasks complete and verified:
- Announce: "I'm using the finishing-a-development-branch skill to complete this work."
- **REQUIRED SUB-SKILL:** Use superpowers:finishing-a-development-branch
- Follow that skill to verify tests, present options, execute choice

## When to Stop and Ask for Help

**STOP executing immediately when:**
- Hit a blocker mid-batch (missing dependency, test fails, instruction unclear)
- Plan has critical gaps preventing starting
- You don't understand an instruction
- Verification fails repeatedly

**Ask for clarification rather than guessing.**

## When to Revisit Earlier Steps

**Return to Review (Step 1) when:**
- Partner updates the plan based on your feedback
- Fundamental approach needs rethinking

**Don't force through blockers** - stop and ask.

## Remember
- Review plan critically first
- Follow plan steps exactly
- Don't skip verifications
- Reference skills when plan says to
- Between batches: just report and wait
- Stop when blocked, don't guess
- Never start implementation on main/master branch without explicit user consent

## Integration

**Required workflow skills:**
- **superpowers:using-git-worktrees** - REQUIRED: Set up isolated workspace before starting
- **superpowers:writing-plans** - Creates the plan this skill executes
- **superpowers:finishing-a-development-branch** - Complete development after all tasks

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user provided a detailed plan for "Luma AMD Speedrun: Phase 2 — VLIW-Informed Custom Kernel Approaches" to optimize three GPU kernels (GEMM, MoE, MLA) for AMD MI355X hardware in a competitive leaderboard setting. The plan had 6 specific tasks:
   - Task 1: GEMM — `get_torch_quant` + manual shuffle JIT bypass
   - Task 2: GEMM — Try `gemm_afp4wfp4` Triton alternative
   - Task 3: MoE — Remote source introspection (print aiter CK configs)
   - Task 4: MoE — Direct CK stage kernel call (bypass fused_moe dispatch)
   - Task 5: MLA — Try MXFP4 KV cache
   - Task 6: Deep source reading — aiter tuned CSV dump
   
   Current leaderboard gaps: GEMM 2.49x behind leader (24us vs 9.7us), MoE 1.28x behind (185us vs 145us), MLA 44x behind (191us vs 4.3us).

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - AMD MI355X GPU kernel optimization via Popcorn CLI remote submission
   - aiter library: ROCm GPU kernel library with JIT compilation (CK and ASM kernels)
   - MXFP4 (Microscaling FP4): 4-bit floating point with E8M0 block-32 scaling
   - `get_triton_quant` vs `get_torch_quant` vs `get_hip_quant`: different quantization backends producing different numerical results
   - `gemm_a4w4` (CK/ASM kernel, needs shuffled inputs) vs `gemm_afp4wfp4` (Triton kernel, needs unshuffled uint8 inputs)
   - `fused_moe` 2-stage CK pipeline: `ck_moe_stage1` + `ck_moe_stage2` with token sorting
   - MLA decode with persistent mode metadata (`get_mla_metadata_v1`)
   - FP8 per-tensor quantization for Q and KV in MLA decode
   - Popcorn CLI workflow: `--mode test` → `--mode benchmark` → `--mode leaderboard`
   - JIT build times consuming ~4 min of 12-min workflow timeout

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`.worktrees/spec-luma-amd-speedrun/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py`** (GEMM kernel)
     - Modified multiple times. Final version is introspection + gemm_afp4wfp4 attempt with ref_kernel fallback.
     - Current content (last written version):
     ```python
     """
     MXFP4 GEMM — Phase 2: Triton gemm_afp4wfp4 discovery.
     """
     import sys
     import aiter
     from aiter import QuantType, dtypes
     from aiter.ops.triton.gemm.basic.gemm_afp4wfp4 import gemm_afp4wfp4
     from task import input_t, output_t
     from reference import ref_kernel

     def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
         A = A.contiguous()
         quant_func = aiter.get_torch_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
         A_q, A_scale = quant_func(A, quant_dtype=dtypes.fp4x2)
         B_q_raw, B_scale_raw = quant_func(B, quant_dtype=dtypes.fp4x2)
         try:
             out = gemm_afp4wfp4(A_q, B_q_raw, A_scale, B_scale_raw, dtype=dtypes.bf16)
             return out
         except Exception as e:
             print(f"GEMM_AFP4WFP4_FAIL: {e}", file=sys.stderr)
             from reference import ref_kernel
             return ref_kernel(data)
     ```

   - **`.worktrees/spec-luma-amd-speedrun/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission.py`** (MoE kernel)
     - Modified to read dsv3_fp4_tuned_fmoe.csv model configs and call get_2stage_cfgs with benchmark shapes. Still delegates to `fused_moe` with reference parameters.
     - Current content reads dsv3 configs on first call, then runs standard fused_moe.

   - **`.worktrees/spec-luma-amd-speedrun/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py`** (MLA kernel)
     - Modified to try MXFP4 KV cache first, falling back to fp8. Clean diagnostics version.
     - Current content tries `_run_mla` with MXFP4 KV data, catches RuntimeError, falls back to fp8 path.

   - **Reference files read** (not modified):
     - `kernels/mxfp4-mm/reference.py`: Shows `get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)` → `quant_func(A, shuffle=True)` → `gemm_a4w4(A_q, B_shuffle, A_scale_sh, B_scale_sh, dtype=bf16, bpreshuffle=True)`
     - `kernels/moe-mxfp4/reference.py`: Shows `fused_moe()` call with `ActivationType.Silu`, `QuantType.per_1x32`, `doweight_stage1=False`. Line 109 uses `get_torch_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)` for weight quantization.
     - `kernels/mixed-mla/reference.py`: Shows fp8 Q + fp8 KV path via `mla_decode_fwd`. Provides three KV formats: bf16, fp8, mxfp4.

4. Errors and fixes:

   - **GEMM Task 1 - torch_quant produces wrong results**: `get_torch_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)` with `shuffle=True` ran without exceptions but produced numerically different results from `get_triton_quant`. Errors like 165 vs 163 (small but exceed rtol=1e-2). Root cause: torch_quant and triton_quant use different rounding/clamping. Fix: Abandoned this approach, moved to gemm_afp4wfp4.

   - **GEMM Task 2 - gemm_afp4wfp4 KeyError**: `gemm_afp4wfp4` failed with `KeyError: 'REDACTED'` on all 4 test shapes. The Triton kernel doesn't support PyTorch's native `torch.REDACTED` dtype — it expects uint8-packed data. Fix: Fell back to ref_kernel (all tests pass).

   - **MLA Task 5 - MXFP4 KV not supported**: `mla_decode_stage1_asm_fwd` requires `head_size == KV.size(3)`. MXFP4 KV has shape `(total_kv, 1, 288)` because fp4 packs 2 values per byte (576/2=288), but the ASM kernel expects 576 in dimension 3. Error: `RuntimeError: mla_decode_stage1_asm_fwd:only support head_size == KV.size(3) for now`. Fix: Fell back to fp8 KV path.

   - **MLA Batch 1 - CSV dump drowned diagnostics**: First MLA submission included CSV dump that produced 10K+ lines of output, hiding the MXFP4 attempt messages. Fix: Rewrote without CSV dump for Batch 2, got clean error messages.

5. Problem Solving:
   
   **Solved (no improvement found):**
   - GEMM: Both `get_torch_quant` bypass and `gemm_afp4wfp4` Triton alternative failed. The fundamental blocker is that only `get_triton_quant` produces correct quantization for `gemm_a4w4`, and `get_triton_quant` has a JIT call-site bug when imported from submission.py. The reference delegation at ~24us is the best achievable without fixing the JIT bug or finding a way to pass uint8-packed data to gemm_afp4wfp4.
   
   - MLA MXFP4 KV: The ASM kernel explicitly rejects MXFP4 packed dimensions. No MXFP4-aware MLA decode kernel exists in this aiter version.
   
   **Extensive introspection data collected:**
   - Full CSV tuning configs for all kernel types (a4w4, a8w8, bf16, fmoe)
   - `ck_moe_stage1` signature: `(hidden_states, w1, w2, sorted_token_ids, sorted_expert_ids, num_valid_ids, out, topk, block_m, a1_scale, w1_scale, kernelName='', sorted_weights=None, quant_type=<QuantType.No: 0>, activation=<ActivationType.Gelu: 1>, splitk=1, ...)`
   - `ck_moe_stage2` JIT signature (not in dir()): `(inter_states, w1, w2, sorted_token_ids, sorted_expert_ids, num_valid_ids, out, topk, kernelName=None, w2_scale=None, a2_scale=None, block_m=32, sorted_weights=None, ...)`
   - `cktile_moe_stage1` and `cktile_moe_stage2` exist in fused_moe module
   - `fused_moe_2stages` exists
   - MXFP4 MoE uses DEFAULT 2-stage configs (no tuned CSV for QuantType.per_1x32)
   - `dsv3_fp4_tuned_fmoe.csv` exists but contains a8w8_blockscale configs, not fp4 MoE configs
   - `gemm_afp4wfp4` signature: `(x, w, x_scales, w_scales, dtype=bf16, y=None, config=None, skip_reduce=False)`
   - Available quant functions: `per_1x32_f4_quant`, `per_1x32_f4_quant_hip`, `per_1x32_f4_quant_triton`, `dynamic_per_group_scaled_quant_fp4`
   - `gemm_a4w4_blockscale` exists as another GEMM variant

6. All user messages:
   - First message: "Implement the following plan:" followed by the complete Phase 2 plan with 6 tasks, execution order, Popcorn CLI reference, risks, and verification criteria.
   - No other non-tool user messages. All subsequent messages were tool results and task notifications.

7. Pending Tasks:
   - **Task 4 (MoE Direct CK stage kernel call)**: Status is "pending" (blocked by Task 3 which is now complete). The introspection revealed `ck_moe_stage1` is callable but `ck_moe_stage2` is only in the JIT module. `cktile_moe_stage1/2` are also available. The MoE submission currently still delegates to standard `fused_moe` — no direct CK call has been attempted yet.
   - **GEMM gemm_afp4wfp4 with uint8 view**: Could try `A_q.view(torch.uint8)` before passing to gemm_afp4wfp4, since the Triton kernel likely expects uint8-packed data rather than `torch.REDACTED` dtype.
   - All submissions should be restored to clean, non-introspection versions for any benchmark/leaderboard submissions.

8. Current Work:
   All 6 plan tasks have been attempted via Popcorn CLI remote submissions (12 total submissions across Batch 1 and Batch 2). Results were being collected from three parallel Batch 2 background submissions:
   
   - GEMM (bdexne7ui): `gemm_afp4wfp4` failed with `KeyError: 'REDACTED'`, fell back to ref_kernel, all 4 tests passed.
   - MoE (boiwo4b28): Read dsv3 model configs (a8w8_blockscale data, not fp4 MoE tuning), fused_moe with defaults, all 3 tests passed.  
   - MLA (bcefq091p): MXFP4 KV failed with `RuntimeError: mla_decode_stage1_asm_fwd:only support head_size == KV.size(3) for now`, fell back to fp8, all 4 tests passed.
   
   Task status: #1 completed, #2 in_progress, #3 completed, #4 pending, #5 completed, #6 in_progress.

9. Optional Next Step:
   The most actionable remaining work is:
   
   a) **Try gemm_afp4wfp4 with uint8 view**: The `KeyError: 'REDACTED'` suggests the Triton kernel needs `uint8`-viewed tensors. Try `A_q.view(torch.uint8)` and `B_q_raw.view(torch.uint8)` before calling `gemm_afp4wfp4`. This is directly in line with Task 2 from the plan.

# /learn - Online Learning System

**Extract reusable knowledge from this session into skills.** Evaluates what was learned, checks for existing skills, and creates new ones when valuable patterns are discovered.

---

## TABLE OF CONTENTS

| Phase       | Description                                            | Steps   |
| ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------ | ------- |
| **Phase 0** | Reference: triggers, quality criteria, skill structure | 0.1–0.4 |
| **Phase 1** | Evaluate: assess if knowledge is worth extracting      | 1.1     |
| **Phase 2** | Check Existing: search for related skills              | 2.1     |
| **Phase 3** | Create Skill: write the skill file                     | 3.1–3.2 |
| **Phase 4** | Quality Gates: final checklist before saving           | 4.1     |

**Quick Step Reference:**

- 0.1 Triggers, 0.2 Quality criteria, 0.3 What NOT to extract, 0.4 Skill structure
- 1.1 Self-evaluation questions
- 2.1 Search existing skills
- 3.1 Choose location, 3.2 Write SKILL.md
- 4.1 Final checklist

---

## PHASE 0: REFERENCE

### Step 0.1: Triggers

Invoke `/learn` after ANY task involving:

| Trigger                      | Example                                                          |
| ---------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Non-obvious debugging**    | Spent 10+ minutes investigating; solution wasn't in docs         |
| **Misleading errors**        | Error message pointed wrong direction; found real cause          |
| **Workarounds**              | Found limitation and creative solution                           |
| **Tool integration**         | Figured out how to use tool/API in undocumented way              |
| **Trial-and-error**          | Tried multiple approaches before finding what worked             |
| **Repeatable workflow**      | Multi-step task that will recur; worth standardizing             |
| **External service queries** | Fetched data from Jira, GitHub, Confluence, or other APIs        |
| **User-facing automation**   | Built something user will ask for again (reports, status checks) |

### Step 0.2: Quality Criteria

Skills must be:

- **Reusable**: Will help with future tasks (not just this instance)
- **Non-trivial**: Required discovery, OR is a valuable workflow pattern
- **Verified**: Solution actually worked, not theoretical

### Step 0.3: What NOT to Extract

- Single-step tasks with no workflow value
- One-off fixes unlikely to recur
- Knowledge easily found in official docs

### Step 0.4: Skill Structure

**Location:** `.claude/skills/[skill-name]/SKILL.md`

**Template:**

```markdown
---
name: descriptive-kebab-case-name
description: |
  [CRITICAL: This determines when the skill triggers. Include:]
  - What the skill does
  - Specific trigger conditions (exact error messages, symptoms)
  - When to use it (contexts, scenarios)
author: Claude Code
version: 1.0.0
---

# Skill Name

## Problem

[Clear description of the problem]

## Context / Trigger Conditions

[When to use - exact error messages, symptoms, scenarios]

## Solution

[Step-by-step solution]

## Verification

[How to verify it worked]

## Example

[Concrete example]

## References

[Links to documentation]
```

<details>
<summary>Writing Effective Descriptions</summary>

The description field is CRITICAL for skill discovery:

**Good:**

```yaml
description: |
  Fix for "ENOENT: no such file or directory" errors in npm monorepos.
  Use when: (1) npm run fails with ENOENT, (2) paths work in root but
  not in packages, (3) symlinked dependencies cause failures.
```

**Bad:**

```yaml
description: Helps with npm problems in monorepos.
```

</details>

**Guidelines:**

- **Concise** - Claude is smart; only add what it doesn't know
- **Under 500 lines** - Move large docs to `references/`
- **Examples over explanations** - Show, don't tell

---

## PHASE 1: EVALUATE

### Step 1.1: Self-Evaluation Questions

Ask yourself:

1. "What did I just learn that wasn't obvious before starting?"
2. "Would future-me benefit from having this documented?"
3. "Was the solution non-obvious from documentation alone?"
4. "Is this a multi-step workflow I'd repeat on similar tasks?"
5. "Did I query an external service the user will ask about again?"

**If NO to all → Skip extraction, nothing to learn.**

**Note:** External service queries (Jira tickets, GitHub PRs, Confluence pages) are almost always worth extracting - users frequently repeat these requests.

---

## PHASE 2: CHECK EXISTING

### Step 2.1: Search for Related Skills

Before creating, search for related skills:

```bash
ls .claude/skills/ 2>/dev/null
rg -i "keyword" .claude/skills/ 2>/dev/null
ls ~/.claude/pilot/skills/ 2>/dev/null
rg -i "keyword" ~/.claude/pilot/skills/ 2>/dev/null
```

| Found                | Action                           |
| -------------------- | -------------------------------- |
| Nothing related      | Create new skill                 |
| Same trigger and fix | Update existing (bump version)   |
| Partial overlap      | Update existing with new variant |

---

## PHASE 3: CREATE SKILL

### Step 3.1: Choose Location

**Project skills**: `.claude/skills/[skill-name]/SKILL.md`

### Step 3.2: Write SKILL.md

Use the template from Step 0.4. Ensure the description field contains specific trigger conditions.

---

## PHASE 4: QUALITY GATES

### Step 4.1: Final Checklist

Before finalizing:

- [ ] Description contains specific trigger conditions
- [ ] Solution verified to work
- [ ] Content specific enough to be actionable
- [ ] Content general enough to be reusable
- [ ] No sensitive information

---

## EXAMPLE

```markdown
---
name: lsp-dead-code-finder
description: |
  Find dead/unused code using LSP findReferences. Use when: (1) user asks
  to find dead code, (2) cleaning up codebase, (3) refactoring. Key insight:
  function with only 1 reference (definition) or only test refs is dead code.
---

# LSP Dead Code Finder

...
```

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **AMD MI355X GPU kernel optimization** via Popcorn CLI remote submission (~3-5 min per submission including JIT build)
   - **MXFP4 (Microscaling FP4)**: 4-bit floating point with E8M0 block-32 scaling; packs 2 fp4 values per byte (`torch.REDACTED`)
   - **JIT call-site sensitivity**: `get_triton_quant` from submission.py produces subtly different quantization than from reference.py — confirmed for BOTH `shuffle=True` AND `shuffle=False` paths

   - **`gemm_a4w4`**: CK/ASM GEMM kernel that needs pre-shuffled inputs and triton_quant output
   - **`fused_moe_2stages`**: Lower-level MoE API requiring pre-sorted tokens (6 extra positional args)
   - **`moe_sorting_fwd`**: JIT-injected function at `aiter.moe_sorting_fwd` (not importable via normal Python import from `aiter.fused_moe`)
   - **`cktile_moe_stage1/stage2`**: Alternative CK tile variants with `split_k`, `n_pad_zeros`, `k_pad_zeros` parameters
   - **Popcorn CLI**: `--mode test` → `--mode benchmark` → `--mode leaderboard`; ~12 min total workflow timeout

     ```python
     """
     MXFP4 MoE — Clean submission (fused_moe with reference parameters).

     Phase 2 findings:
     - fused_moe_2stages requires pre-sorted tokens (6 extra positional args)
     - moe_sorting_fwd lives at aiter.moe_sorting_fwd (not aiter.fused_moe)
     - Direct 2-stage call failed: isG1U1 positional arg conflict + GPU memory fault
     - cktile_moe_stage1/stage2 exist but need same sorting setup
     - No tuned CSV for QuantType.per_1x32 — uses default 2-stage configs

     The standard fused_moe with reference parameters is the safest path.
     MoE gap is only 1.28x — the kernel is already well-optimized.
     """
     from task import input_t, output_t
     from aiter import ActivationType, QuantType
     from aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe

     def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         (
             hidden_states, gate_up_weight, down_weight,
             gate_up_weight_scale, down_weight_scale,
             gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,
             gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled, down_weight_scale_shuffled,
             topk_weights, topk_ids, config,
         ) = data

         hidden_pad = config["d_hidden_pad"] - config["d_hidden"]
         intermediate_pad = config["d_expert_pad"] - config["d_expert"]

         output = fused_moe(
             hidden_states,
             gate_up_weight_shuffled,
             down_weight_shuffled,
             topk_weights,
             topk_ids,
             expert_mask=None,
             activation=ActivationType.Silu,
             quant_type=QuantType.per_1x32,
             doweight_stage1=False,
             w1_scale=gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
             w2_scale=down_weight_scale_shuffled,
             a1_scale=None,
             a2_scale=None,
             hidden_pad=hidden_pad,
             intermediate_pad=intermediate_pad,
         )

         return output
     ```

4. Errors and Fixes:
   - **GEMM: `KeyError: 'REDACTED'` in gemm_afp4wfp4** (from prior session):
     - Fix: View data tensors as `torch.uint8` before passing to gemm_afp4wfp4
     - This revealed a second KeyError: `KeyError: 'REDACTED'` for E8M0 scale dtype
     - Fix: View BOTH data AND scales as uint8: `A_q.view(torch.uint8)`, `A_scale.view(torch.uint8)`
     - Kernel ran successfully but produced wrong results (quantization mismatch)

   - **GEMM: Wrong results from gemm_afp4wfp4 with uint8 views** (622,663 mismatched elements):
     - Root cause: `get_torch_quant` produces different quantization than `get_triton_quant`
     - The GEMM kernel itself works fine with uint8 views — the input quantization is wrong
     - No fix possible without using `get_triton_quant`, which has the JIT call-site bug

   - **GEMM: `get_triton_quant(shuffle=True)` from submission.py produces wrong results**:
     - Confirmed: JIT call-site bug affects ALL `get_triton_quant` paths (both shuffle=True and shuffle=False)
     - No fix — fundamental limitation of Triton JIT in multiprocessing.Pool(spawn) context
     - Reverted to `ref_kernel` delegation

   - **MoE: `ImportError: cannot import name 'moe_sorting_fwd' from 'aiter.fused_moe'`**:
     - Root cause: `moe_sorting_fwd` is JIT-compiled and injected at runtime into `aiter` package namespace
     - Fix: Access via `aiter.moe_sorting_fwd` after triggering JIT build with initial `fused_moe` call
     - Successfully found the function after JIT build

   - **MoE: `TypeError: fused_moe_2stages() got multiple values for argument 'isG1U1'`**:
     - Root cause: Positional argument ordering mismatch — `topk_weights` and `topk_ids` occupy positions that conflict with keyword `isG1U1`
     - No fix attempted — would require reading full source to determine exact positional order
     - Fallback to `fused_moe` still works

   - **MoE: `Memory access fault by GPU node-2`**:
     - Root cause: Incorrect sorting buffer allocation or `unit_size=1` parameter wrong
     - The direct 2-stage call corrupted GPU state, crashing subsequent fallback calls too
     - Fix: Reverted to standard `fused_moe` which handles buffer allocation internally

5. Problem Solving:
   **Solved (all dead ends, reverted to safe paths):**
   - GEMM JIT call-site bug: Confirmed affects ALL `get_triton_quant` paths. Only `ref_kernel` delegation works (~24us).
   - GEMM gemm_afp4wfp4: Works with uint8 views but wrong results from quantization mismatch.
   - MoE fused_moe_2stages: Requires pre-sorted tokens with exact buffer sizing. Positional arg conflict. GPU memory fault on incorrect allocation.

   - `cktile_moe_stage2` signature: `(a2, w1, w2, sorted_token_ids, sorted_expert_ids, num_valid_ids, out, topk, w2_scale, a2_scale, block_m, activation=Swiglu, sorted_weights=None, zeros_out=False, n_pad_zeros=0, k_pad_zeros=0, bias2=None)`

# /learn - Online Learning System

**Extract reusable knowledge from this session into skills.** Evaluates what was learned, checks for existing skills, and creates new ones when valuable patterns are discovered.

---

## TABLE OF CONTENTS

| Phase       | Description                                            | Steps   |
| ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------ | ------- |
| **Phase 0** | Reference: triggers, quality criteria, skill structure | 0.1–0.4 |
| **Phase 1** | Evaluate: assess if knowledge is worth extracting      | 1.1     |
| **Phase 2** | Check Existing: search for related skills              | 2.1     |
| **Phase 3** | Create Skill: write the skill file                     | 3.1–3.2 |
| **Phase 4** | Quality Gates: final checklist before saving           | 4.1     |

**Quick Step Reference:**

- 0.1 Triggers, 0.2 Quality criteria, 0.3 What NOT to extract, 0.4 Skill structure
- 1.1 Self-evaluation questions
- 2.1 Search existing skills
- 3.1 Choose location, 3.2 Write SKILL.md
- 4.1 Final checklist

---

## PHASE 0: REFERENCE

### Step 0.1: Triggers

Invoke `/learn` after ANY task involving:

| Trigger                      | Example                                                          |
| ---------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Non-obvious debugging**    | Spent 10+ minutes investigating; solution wasn't in docs         |
| **Misleading errors**        | Error message pointed wrong direction; found real cause          |
| **Workarounds**              | Found limitation and creative solution                           |
| **Tool integration**         | Figured out how to use tool/API in undocumented way              |
| **Trial-and-error**          | Tried multiple approaches before finding what worked             |
| **Repeatable workflow**      | Multi-step task that will recur; worth standardizing             |
| **External service queries** | Fetched data from Jira, GitHub, Confluence, or other APIs        |
| **User-facing automation**   | Built something user will ask for again (reports, status checks) |

### Step 0.2: Quality Criteria

Skills must be:

- **Reusable**: Will help with future tasks (not just this instance)
- **Non-trivial**: Required discovery, OR is a valuable workflow pattern
- **Verified**: Solution actually worked, not theoretical

### Step 0.3: What NOT to Extract

- Single-step tasks with no workflow value
- One-off fixes unlikely to recur
- Knowledge easily found in official docs

### Step 0.4: Skill Structure

**Location:** `.claude/skills/[skill-name]/SKILL.md`

**Template:**

```markdown
---
name: descriptive-kebab-case-name
description: |
  [CRITICAL: This determines when the skill triggers. Include:]
  - What the skill does
  - Specific trigger conditions (exact error messages, symptoms)
  - When to use it (contexts, scenarios)
author: Claude Code
version: 1.0.0
---

# Skill Name

## Problem

[Clear description of the problem]

## Context / Trigger Conditions

[When to use - exact error messages, symptoms, scenarios]

## Solution

[Step-by-step solution]

## Verification

[How to verify it worked]

## Example

[Concrete example]

## References

[Links to documentation]
```

<details>
<summary>Writing Effective Descriptions</summary>

The description field is CRITICAL for skill discovery:

**Good:**

```yaml
description: |
  Fix for "ENOENT: no such file or directory" errors in npm monorepos.
  Use when: (1) npm run fails with ENOENT, (2) paths work in root but
  not in packages, (3) symlinked dependencies cause failures.
```

**Bad:**

```yaml
description: Helps with npm problems in monorepos.
```

</details>

**Guidelines:**

- **Concise** - Claude is smart; only add what it doesn't know
- **Under 500 lines** - Move large docs to `references/`
- **Examples over explanations** - Show, don't tell

---

## PHASE 1: EVALUATE

### Step 1.1: Self-Evaluation Questions

Ask yourself:

1. "What did I just learn that wasn't obvious before starting?"
2. "Would future-me benefit from having this documented?"
3. "Was the solution non-obvious from documentation alone?"
4. "Is this a multi-step workflow I'd repeat on similar tasks?"
5. "Did I query an external service the user will ask about again?"

**If NO to all → Skip extraction, nothing to learn.**

**Note:** External service queries (Jira tickets, GitHub PRs, Confluence pages) are almost always worth extracting - users frequently repeat these requests.

---

## PHASE 2: CHECK EXISTING

### Step 2.1: Search for Related Skills

Before creating, search for related skills:

```bash
ls .claude/skills/ 2>/dev/null
rg -i "keyword" .claude/skills/ 2>/dev/null
ls ~/.claude/pilot/skills/ 2>/dev/null
rg -i "keyword" ~/.claude/pilot/skills/ 2>/dev/null
```

| Found                | Action                           |
| -------------------- | -------------------------------- |
| Nothing related      | Create new skill                 |
| Same trigger and fix | Update existing (bump version)   |
| Partial overlap      | Update existing with new variant |

**Project skills**: `.claude/skills/[skill-name]/SKILL.md`

### Step 3.2: Write SKILL.md

Use the template from Step 0.4. Ensure the description field contains specific trigger conditions.

## PHASE 4: QUALITY GATES

### Step 4.1: Final Checklist

Before finalizing:

- [ ] Description contains specific trigger conditions
- [ ] Solution verified to work
- [ ] Content specific enough to be actionable
- [ ] Content general enough to be reusable
- [ ] No sensitive information

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user is competing in the Luma AMD Speedrun competition, optimizing three GPU kernels (GEMM, MoE, MLA) for AMD MI355X hardware. After Phase 2 exhausted all known API-level approaches (all tasks completed, all dead ends), the user asked to:
   1. Run `/learn` to extract reusable knowledge from the session
   2. "Pick the path that unlocks elegantly simple compound engineering solutions with adequate context awareness that optimizes token efficiencies. Remember not all tokens are created equal." — a strategic directive to find the highest-ROI optimization path forward.

   Current leaderboard gaps: GEMM 2.49x behind (24us vs 9.7us), MoE 1.28x behind (185us vs 145us), MLA 44x behind (191us vs 4.3us).

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **AMD MI355X GPU kernel optimization** via Popcorn CLI remote submission (~5-10 min per submission including JIT build)
   - **MXFP4 (Microscaling FP4)**: 4-bit floating point with E8M0 block-32 scaling; packs 2 fp4 values per byte (`torch.REDACTED`)
   - **JIT call-site sensitivity**: `get_triton_quant` from submission.py produces wrong results. Confirmed: both shuffle=True/False fail AND JIT cache warmup does NOT fix it (per-call-site issue, not per-cache)
   - **`get_hip_quant`**: Compiled C kernel (NOT Triton JIT) — discovered as the internal quantization backend used by `fused_moe.py`. May NOT have the JIT callsite sensitivity bug.
   - **`fused_dynamic_mxfp4_quant_moe_sort`**: Fused Triton kernel combining MXFP4 activation quantization + MoE token sorting in one pass
   - **`fused_moe_1stage_dict`**: gfx950 entry exists for `(SiLu, per_1x32, bf16, fp4x2, fp4x2, True, False) → aiter.fmoe_g1u1` but `run_1stage=False` by default — the 1-stage kernel exists but is never selected for per_1x32
   - **Environment variables**: `AITER_KSPLIT` (K-dim splitting), `AITER_USE_NT` (non-temporal loads), `AITER_ONLINE_TUNE` (online auto-tuning), `AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG`
   - **`get_block_size_M`**: Occupancy-based heuristic selecting from [32, 64, 128] based on CU utilization
   - **Popcorn CLI**: `--mode test` → `--mode benchmark` → `--mode leaderboard`; ~12 min total workflow timeout

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`~/.claude/skills/amd-triton-jit-callsite-correctness/SKILL.md`** (UPDATED to v1.2.0)
     - Added "Confirmed: shuffle=False Also Fails" section
     - Added "Confirmed: JIT Cache Warmup Does NOT Fix It" section with test results (89-95% mismatch even after ref_kernel warms JIT)
     - Key finding: issue is per-call-site, not per-JIT-cache

   - **`~/.claude/skills/competitive-kernel-optimization-ceiling/SKILL.md`** (UPDATED)
     - Added "Phase 2 Exhausted Paths" table with all 7 dead-end approaches and specific blockers
     - Added GEMM JIT warmup test to the table

   - **`kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py`** (CURRENT STATE — hip_quant test, NOT YET SUBMITTED):
     ```python
     """
     MXFP4 GEMM — Phase 3: get_hip_quant test.
     Key insight from reading fused_moe.py source:
       Line 15: from aiter import get_hip_quant as get_quant
       fused_moe internally uses get_hip_quant, NOT get_triton_quant!
       HIP quant is a compiled C kernel, not Triton JIT — may not have
       the callsite sensitivity bug.
     Test: use get_hip_quant for A quantization, then gemm_a4w4.
     Falls back to ref_kernel if correctness fails.
     """
     import sys
     import torch
     from task import input_t, output_t
     from reference import ref_kernel
     import aiter
     from aiter import QuantType, dtypes

     def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
         try:
             quant_func = aiter.get_hip_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
             A_cont = A.contiguous()
             A_q, A_scale_sh = quant_func(A_cont, shuffle=True)
             our_out = aiter.gemm_a4w4(
                 A_q, B_shuffle, A_scale_sh, B_scale_sh,
                 dtype=dtypes.bf16, bpreshuffle=True,
             )
             ref_out = ref_kernel(data)
             diff = (our_out - ref_out).abs()
             max_err = diff.max().item()
             mismatch = (diff > 1e-2).sum().item()
             total = diff.numel()
             print(f"HIP_QUANT_TEST: max_err={max_err:.6f}, "
                   f"mismatched={mismatch}/{total} ({100*mismatch/total:.1f}%)",
                   file=sys.stderr)
             if max_err < 1e-2:
                 print("HIP_QUANT_RESULT: SUCCESS — hip_quant works from submission.py!",
                       file=sys.stderr)
                 return our_out
             else:
                 print("HIP_QUANT_RESULT: FAIL — hip_quant also has callsite issue",
                       file=sys.stderr)
                 return ref_out
         except Exception as e:
             print(f"HIP_QUANT_ERROR: {type(e).__name__}: {e}", file=sys.stderr)
             return ref_kernel(data)
     ```

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission.py`** (CURRENT STATE — diagnostic, lines 450-900 reader):
     ```python
     """
     MXFP4 MoE — Phase 3b: Read fused_moe_2stages + get_2stage_cfgs source.
     """
     import sys
     import inspect
     from task import input_t, output_t
     from reference import ref_kernel

     def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         try:
             from aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe
             source_file = inspect.getfile(fused_moe)
             with open(source_file) as f:
                 lines = f.readlines()
             for i in range(449, min(899, len(lines))):
                 print(f"{i+1:4d}| {lines[i].rstrip()}", file=sys.stderr)
         except Exception as e:
             print(f"SOURCE_READ_ERROR: {type(e).__name__}: {e}", file=sys.stderr)
         try:
             from aiter.ops.triton.quant.fused_mxfp4_quant import fused_dynamic_mxfp4_quant_moe_sort
             sig = inspect.signature(fused_dynamic_mxfp4_quant_moe_sort)
             print(f"\nFUSED_QUANT_SORT_SIG: {sig}", file=sys.stderr)
             src = inspect.getsource(fused_dynamic_mxfp4_quant_moe_sort)
             src_lines = src.split('\n')
             print(f"FUSED_QUANT_SORT_LINES: {len(src_lines)}", file=sys.stderr)
             for i, line in enumerate(src_lines[:100]):
                 print(f"FQS {i+1:3d}| {line}", file=sys.stderr)
         except Exception as e:
             print(f"FUSED_QUANT_SORT_ERROR: {type(e).__name__}: {e}", file=sys.stderr)
         return ref_kernel(data)
     ```

# /learn - Online Learning System

**Extract reusable knowledge from this session into skills.** Evaluates what was learned, checks for existing skills, and creates new ones when valuable patterns are discovered.

---

## TABLE OF CONTENTS

| Phase       | Description                                            | Steps   |
| ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------ | ------- |
| **Phase 0** | Reference: triggers, quality criteria, skill structure | 0.1–0.4 |
| **Phase 1** | Evaluate: assess if knowledge is worth extracting      | 1.1     |
| **Phase 2** | Check Existing: search for related skills              | 2.1     |
| **Phase 3** | Create Skill: write the skill file                     | 3.1–3.2 |
| **Phase 4** | Quality Gates: final checklist before saving           | 4.1     |

**Quick Step Reference:**

- 0.1 Triggers, 0.2 Quality criteria, 0.3 What NOT to extract, 0.4 Skill structure
- 1.1 Self-evaluation questions
- 2.1 Search existing skills
- 3.1 Choose location, 3.2 Write SKILL.md
- 4.1 Final checklist

---

## PHASE 0: REFERENCE

### Step 0.1: Triggers

Invoke `/learn` after ANY task involving:

| Trigger                      | Example                                                          |
| ---------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Non-obvious debugging**    | Spent 10+ minutes investigating; solution wasn't in docs         |
| **Misleading errors**        | Error message pointed wrong direction; found real cause          |
| **Workarounds**              | Found limitation and creative solution                           |
| **Tool integration**         | Figured out how to use tool/API in undocumented way              |
| **Trial-and-error**          | Tried multiple approaches before finding what worked             |
| **Repeatable workflow**      | Multi-step task that will recur; worth standardizing             |
| **External service queries** | Fetched data from Jira, GitHub, Confluence, or other APIs        |
| **User-facing automation**   | Built something user will ask for again (reports, status checks) |

### Step 0.2: Quality Criteria

Skills must be:

- **Reusable**: Will help with future tasks (not just this instance)
- **Non-trivial**: Required discovery, OR is a valuable workflow pattern
- **Verified**: Solution actually worked, not theoretical

### Step 0.3: What NOT to Extract

- Single-step tasks with no workflow value
- One-off fixes unlikely to recur
- Knowledge easily found in official docs

### Step 0.4: Skill Structure

**Location:** `.claude/skills/[skill-name]/SKILL.md`

**Template:**

```markdown
---
name: descriptive-kebab-case-name
description: |
  [CRITICAL: This determines when the skill triggers. Include:]
  - What the skill does
  - Specific trigger conditions (exact error messages, symptoms)
  - When to use it (contexts, scenarios)
author: Claude Code
version: 1.0.0
---

# Skill Name

## Problem

[Clear description of the problem]

## Context / Trigger Conditions

[When to use - exact error messages, symptoms, scenarios]

## Solution

[Step-by-step solution]

## Verification

[How to verify it worked]

## Example

[Concrete example]

## References

[Links to documentation]
```

<details>
<summary>Writing Effective Descriptions</summary>

The description field is CRITICAL for skill discovery:

**Good:**

```yaml
description: |
  Fix for "ENOENT: no such file or directory" errors in npm monorepos.
  Use when: (1) npm run fails with ENOENT, (2) paths work in root but
  not in packages, (3) symlinked dependencies cause failures.
```

**Bad:**

```yaml
description: Helps with npm problems in monorepos.
```

</details>

**Guidelines:**

- **Concise** - Claude is smart; only add what it doesn't know
- **Under 500 lines** - Move large docs to `references/`
- **Examples over explanations** - Show, don't tell

---

## PHASE 1: EVALUATE

### Step 1.1: Self-Evaluation Questions

Ask yourself:

1. "What did I just learn that wasn't obvious before starting?"
2. "Would future-me benefit from having this documented?"
3. "Was the solution non-obvious from documentation alone?"
4. "Is this a multi-step workflow I'd repeat on similar tasks?"
5. "Did I query an external service the user will ask about again?"

**If NO to all → Skip extraction, nothing to learn.**

**Note:** External service queries (Jira tickets, GitHub PRs, Confluence pages) are almost always worth extracting - users frequently repeat these requests.

---

## PHASE 2: CHECK EXISTING

### Step 2.1: Search for Related Skills

Before creating, search for related skills:

```bash
ls .claude/skills/ 2>/dev/null
rg -i "keyword" .claude/skills/ 2>/dev/null
ls ~/.claude/pilot/skills/ 2>/dev/null
rg -i "keyword" ~/.claude/pilot/skills/ 2>/dev/null
```

| Found                | Action                           |
| -------------------- | -------------------------------- |
| Nothing related      | Create new skill                 |
| Same trigger and fix | Update existing (bump version)   |
| Partial overlap      | Update existing with new variant |

---

## PHASE 3: CREATE SKILL

### Step 3.1: Choose Location

**Project skills**: `.claude/skills/[skill-name]/SKILL.md`

### Step 3.2: Write SKILL.md

Use the template from Step 0.4. Ensure the description field contains specific trigger conditions.

---

## PHASE 4: QUALITY GATES

### Step 4.1: Final Checklist

Before finalizing:

- [ ] Description contains specific trigger conditions
- [ ] Solution verified to work
- [ ] Content specific enough to be actionable
- [ ] Content general enough to be reusable
- [ ] No sensitive information

---

```markdown
name: lsp-dead-code-finder

  to find dead code, (2) cleaning up codebase, (3) refactoring. Key insight:
  function with only 1 reference (definition) or only test refs is dead code.
---

# LSP Dead Code Finder

...
```

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user is competing in the Luma AMD Speedrun competition, optimizing three GPU kernels (GEMM, MoE, MLA) for AMD MI355X. The session was triggered by `/compact` and `/learn` commands. The user's prior strategic directive was: "Pick the path that unlocks elegantly simple compound engineering solutions with adequate context awareness that optimizes token efficiencies. Remember not all tokens are created equal." The assistant followed this by pursuing the highest-ROI optimization paths: testing `get_hip_quant` for GEMM JIT bypass, testing direct `fused_moe` call from submission.py, and K-split tuning for MoE.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **JIT callsite sensitivity is per-DIRECT-caller, NOT per-full-callstack**: When submission.py calls `fused_moe()`, the JIT-sensitive kernels inside fused_moe.py are called with fused_moe.py as their direct caller → WORKS. When submission.py directly calls `gemm_a4w4_asm` → FAILS.
   - **Root cause identified: `gemm_a4w4_asm`** is the callsite-sensitive component, NOT quantization functions (triton, hip, or torch). Both `get_triton_quant` and `get_hip_quant` produce identical failure patterns (81-95% mismatch, 6-14 max error).
   - **AITER_KSPLIT switches kernel paths**: KSPLIT=0 uses `module_moe_ck2stages_fp4x2_fp4x2_preshuffle_*` (MXFP4-optimized CK kernels); KSPLIT>0 switches to `module_moe_cktile2stages` (generic cktile kernels). KSPLIT is IGNORED for CSV-tuned shapes (257-expert configs).
   - **Adaptive KSPLIT**: cktile path is faster for sparse token distributions (estimated_m_per_expert < ~50), while MXFP4-optimized CK path is faster for dense distributions (estimated_m > ~50).
   - **Kernel signatures discovered**:
     - `ck_moe_stage1(hidden_states, w1, w2, sorted_token_ids, sorted_expert_ids, num_valid_ids, out, topk, kernelName=None, w1_scale=None, a1_scale=None, block_m=32, sorted_weights=None, quant_type=0, activation=0, splitk=1, non_temporal_load=False, dst_type=None, is_shuffled=True)`
     - `ck_moe_stage2(inter_states, w1, w2, ...)` — same pattern
     - `moe_sorting_fwd(topk_ids, topk_weights, sorted_token_ids, sorted_weights, sorted_expert_ids, num_valid_ids, moe_buf, num_experts, unit_size, ...)`
     - `cktile_moe_gemm1(XQ, WQ, Y, sorted_ids, sorted_expert_ids, max_token_ids, topk, n_padded_zeros=0, k_padded_zeros=0, topk_weight=None, x_scale=None, w_scale=None, exp_bias=None, activation=0, block_m=32, split_k=1)`
   - **Benchmark shapes** (7 configurations, all dhidden=7168):
     - 3 shapes with 257 experts (256 routed + 1 shared), dexpert=256 — CSV-tuned
     - 3 shapes with 33 experts (32 routed + 1 shared), dexpert=512 — default configs
     - 1 shape with 33 experts, dexpert=2048 — default config, slowest shape (352 µs)

   - **`~/.claude/skills/amd-triton-jit-callsite-correctness/SKILL.md`** (v1.2.0 → v1.3.0)

   - **`kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py`** — Still has hip_quant test code (NEEDS REVERT):
     ```python
     """MXFP4 GEMM — Phase 3: get_hip_quant test."""
     import sys, torch
     from task import input_t, output_t
     from reference import ref_kernel
     import aiter
     from aiter import QuantType, dtypes

     def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
         try:
             quant_func = aiter.get_hip_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
             A_cont = A.contiguous()
             A_q, A_scale_sh = quant_func(A_cont, shuffle=True)
             our_out = aiter.gemm_a4w4(A_q, B_shuffle, A_scale_sh, B_scale_sh, dtype=dtypes.bf16, bpreshuffle=True)
             ref_out = ref_kernel(data)
     ```

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission.py`** — CURRENT: Adaptive KSPLIT (NOT YET TESTED):
     ```python
     """MXFP4 MoE — Phase 6: Adaptive K-split based on estimated tokens per expert."""
     import os
     from task import input_t, output_t
     from aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe
     from aiter import ActivationType, QuantType

     def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         (hidden_states, gate_up_weight, down_weight,
          gate_up_weight_scale, down_weight_scale,
          gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,
          gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled, down_weight_scale_shuffled,
          topk_weights, topk_ids, config) = data

         hidden_pad = config["d_hidden_pad"] - config["d_hidden"]
         intermediate_pad = config["d_expert_pad"] - config["d_expert"]

         num_experts = gate_up_weight_shuffled.shape[0]
         estimated_m = topk_ids.numel() // num_experts

         if estimated_m < 50:
             os.environ["AITER_KSPLIT"] = "2"
         else:
             os.environ.pop("AITER_KSPLIT", None)

         return fused_moe(
             hidden_states, gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,
             topk_weights, topk_ids, expert_mask=None,
             activation=ActivationType.Silu, quant_type=QuantType.per_1x32,
             doweight_stage1=False,
             w1_scale=gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
             w2_scale=down_weight_scale_shuffled,
             a1_scale=None, a2_scale=None,
             hidden_pad=hidden_pad, intermediate_pad=intermediate_pad)
     ```

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission_ksplit4.py`** — Created for parallel KSPLIT=4 testing (can be cleaned up)

4. Errors and Fixes:
   - **GEMM `get_hip_quant` produces wrong results** (95.5%, 94.7%, 93.1%, 81.0% mismatch):
     - Root cause: `gemm_a4w4_asm` JIT dispatch is callsite-sensitive, NOT the quantization function
     - Fix: Accept `ref_kernel` delegation as the GEMM ceiling
   - **First MoE benchmark had comparison overhead** (called both fused_moe AND ref_kernel per invocation):
     - Fix: Created clean production version without comparison logic, re-benchmarked
   - **AITER_KSPLIT=2 catastrophically slows dense shapes** (703 µs vs 352 µs baseline):
     - Root cause: KSPLIT switches from MXFP4-optimized CK kernel to generic cktile kernel
     - Fix: Adaptive KSPLIT based on estimated_m_per_expert threshold (< 50 → KSPLIT=2, else none)

5. Problem Solving:

   **Solved:**
   - JIT callsite sensitivity is in `gemm_a4w4_asm`, not quantization — closes GEMM custom pipeline
   - Direct `fused_moe` call from submission.py WORKS — confirms per-direct-caller sensitivity model
   - AITER_KSPLIT behavior fully characterized: switches kernel paths, good for sparse, bad for dense
   - All 7 benchmark shape configurations documented with auto-tuner selections (block_m, use_nt, ksplit, kernel names)

   **In Progress:**
   - Adaptive KSPLIT written but not yet tested/benchmarked (expected ~168 µs, 9% improvement)

   **Remaining gap:**
   - Current MoE: ~184 µs geomean, leader: ~145 µs, gap: 1.27x
   - Expected after adaptive KSPLIT: ~168 µs, gap: 1.16x
   - GEMM: fully closed at ref_kernel (24 µs, 2.49x gap to leader at 9.7 µs)
   - MLA: no viable path (191 µs, 44x gap to leader at 4.3 µs)

   - `/compact` — triggered context compaction
   - `/learn` — triggered knowledge extraction to create/update reusable skills
   - 6 task completion notifications for background Popcorn CLI submissions (benlk6sov, b2yyx1i99, b24uno650, biromrld8, bz5fz67eo, b37f2s6aw)
   - Final message: "Your task is to create a detailed summary of the conversation so far..."

   - **Benchmark adaptive KSPLIT** — `--mode benchmark` to measure ~168 µs expected geomean
   - **Submit to leaderboard** if benchmark shows improvement over current ranking
   - **Revert GEMM submission.py** to simple `ref_kernel` delegation (currently has hip_quant test code)
   - **Clean up** `submission_ksplit4.py` temporary file

8. Current Work:
   Immediately before the summary request, I wrote the adaptive KSPLIT MoE submission (`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission.py`) which dynamically sets `AITER_KSPLIT=2` for sparse shapes (estimated_m_per_expert < 50) and clears it for dense shapes. This was based on comprehensive benchmark data showing:
   - KSPLIT=2 is 16-36% faster for sparse shapes (bs=16/128 with 33 experts)
   - KSPLIT=2 is 18-100% slower for dense shapes (bs=512 with 33 experts)
   - KSPLIT has no effect on 257-expert CSV-tuned shapes

   The adaptive submission was written to disk but has NOT been submitted to Popcorn CLI for test or benchmark.

   Per-shape expected performance with adaptive KSPLIT:
   | Shape | Expected µs | Source |
   |-------|-------------|--------|
   | bs=16, 257 exp | 140 | CSV-tuned (unchanged) |
   | bs=128, 257 exp | 219 | CSV-tuned (unchanged) |
   | bs=512, 257 exp | 251 | CSV-tuned (unchanged) |
   | bs=16, 33 exp | 60.6 | KSPLIT=2 (from benchmark data) |
   | bs=128, 33 exp | 109 | KSPLIT=2 (from benchmark data) |
   | bs=512, 33 exp d=512 | 214 | No KSPLIT (baseline) |
   | bs=512, 33 exp d=2048 | 352 | No KSPLIT (baseline) |

The test phase will verify correctness (the cktile path uses different accumulation order), and the benchmark will confirm whether we achieve the expected ~168 µs geomean (9% improvement over 184 µs baseline).

   Note: There is a concern that the cktile path with KSPLIT>0 triggers building `module_moe_cktile2stages` (~110s) AND `module_activation` (~22s) JIT modules in addition to the normal modules. This adds ~132s of JIT build time, which may push total workflow close to the 12-minute timeout. The benchmark showed this was manageable, but the test + benchmark sequence should be monitored for timeout risk.

| Phase       | Description                                            | Steps   |

| **Phase 3** | Create Skill: write the skill file                     | 3.1–3.2 |
| **Phase 4** | Quality Gates: final checklist before saving           | 4.1     |

**Quick Step Reference:**

- 0.1 Triggers, 0.2 Quality criteria, 0.3 What NOT to extract, 0.4 Skill structure
- 1.1 Self-evaluation questions
- 2.1 Search existing skills
- 3.1 Choose location, 3.2 Write SKILL.md
- 4.1 Final checklist

---

## PHASE 0: REFERENCE

### Step 0.1: Triggers

Invoke `/learn` after ANY task involving:

| Trigger                      | Example                                                          |
| ---------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Non-obvious debugging**    | Spent 10+ minutes investigating; solution wasn't in docs         |
| **Misleading errors**        | Error message pointed wrong direction; found real cause          |
| **Workarounds**              | Found limitation and creative solution                           |
| **Tool integration**         | Figured out how to use tool/API in undocumented way              |
| **Trial-and-error**          | Tried multiple approaches before finding what worked             |
| **Repeatable workflow**      | Multi-step task that will recur; worth standardizing             |
| **External service queries** | Fetched data from Jira, GitHub, Confluence, or other APIs        |
| **User-facing automation**   | Built something user will ask for again (reports, status checks) |

### Step 0.2: Quality Criteria

Skills must be:

- **Reusable**: Will help with future tasks (not just this instance)
- **Non-trivial**: Required discovery, OR is a valuable workflow pattern
- **Verified**: Solution actually worked, not theoretical

### Step 0.3: What NOT to Extract

- Single-step tasks with no workflow value
- One-off fixes unlikely to recur
- Knowledge easily found in official docs

### Step 0.4: Skill Structure

**Location:** `.claude/skills/[skill-name]/SKILL.md`

**Template:**

```markdown
---
name: descriptive-kebab-case-name
description: |
  [CRITICAL: This determines when the skill triggers. Include:]
  - What the skill does
  - Specific trigger conditions (exact error messages, symptoms)
  - When to use it (contexts, scenarios)
author: Claude Code
version: 1.0.0
---

# Skill Name

## Problem

[Clear description of the problem]

## Context / Trigger Conditions

[When to use - exact error messages, symptoms, scenarios]

## Solution

[Step-by-step solution]

## Verification

[How to verify it worked]

## Example

[Concrete example]

## References

[Links to documentation]
```

<details>
<summary>Writing Effective Descriptions</summary>

The description field is CRITICAL for skill discovery:

**Good:**

```yaml
description: |
  Fix for "ENOENT: no such file or directory" errors in npm monorepos.
  Use when: (1) npm run fails with ENOENT, (2) paths work in root but
  not in packages, (3) symlinked dependencies cause failures.
```

**Bad:**

```yaml
description: Helps with npm problems in monorepos.
```

</details>

**Guidelines:**

- **Concise** - Claude is smart; only add what it doesn't know
- **Under 500 lines** - Move large docs to `references/`
- **Examples over explanations** - Show, don't tell

---

## PHASE 1: EVALUATE

### Step 1.1: Self-Evaluation Questions

Ask yourself:

1. "What did I just learn that wasn't obvious before starting?"
2. "Would future-me benefit from having this documented?"
3. "Was the solution non-obvious from documentation alone?"
4. "Is this a multi-step workflow I'd repeat on similar tasks?"
5. "Did I query an external service the user will ask about again?"

**If NO to all → Skip extraction, nothing to learn.**

**Note:** External service queries (Jira tickets, GitHub PRs, Confluence pages) are almost always worth extracting - users frequently repeat these requests.

| Partial overlap      | Update existing with new variant |

- [ ] Content specific enough to be actionable
- [ ] Content general enough to be reusable
- [ ] No sensitive information

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user is competing in the Luma AMD Speedrun competition, optimizing GPU kernels for AMD MI355X. The session continued from a compacted conversation. Key user directives were:
   - `/learn` — Extract reusable knowledge from the session
   - "Continue" — Execute pending work (test/benchmark/leaderboard the adaptive KSPLIT MoE)
   - "Where are we on the leader board?" — Check current standings
   - **"Well we obviously aren't at the top. I believe in you. Resume your plan."** — Push harder for better MoE performance, continue the plan at `adaptive-baking-globe.md`

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **AITER_KSPLIT kernel path switching**: KSPLIT=0 uses MXFP4-optimized CK kernels; KSPLIT>0 switches to generic cktile2stages kernels. CSV-tuned 257-expert shapes IGNORE AITER_KSPLIT entirely.
   - **Adaptive KSPLIT threshold**: `estimated_m = topk_ids.numel() // num_experts`; if < 50, use KSPLIT=2 (cktile), else clear KSPLIT (CK path)
   - **Stderr diagnostic pattern**: `using 2stage (kernelName1=...)` = CSV-tuned (KSPLIT ignored); `using 2stage default for (...)` = default path (KSPLIT applies)
   - **JIT callsite sensitivity**: `gemm_a4w4_asm` is callsite-sensitive per-direct-caller, making GEMM custom pipelines impossible from submission.py
   - **Popcorn CLI submission workflow**: test → benchmark → leaderboard, ~5-8 min per submission due to JIT builds
   - **AITER_ONLINE_TUNE**: Environment variable that may enable runtime auto-tuning for shapes without CSV configs
   - **tuned_fmoe.csv vs dsv3_fp4_tuned_fmoe.csv**: MoE tuning configs; the dsv3_fp4 variant contains the MXFP4-specific DeepSeek configs used by our 257-expert shapes

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission.py`** — Production adaptive KSPLIT submission (submitted to leaderboard at **166 µs**)
     ```python
     """MXFP4 MoE — Phase 6: Adaptive K-split based on estimated tokens per expert."""
     import os
     from task import input_t, output_t
     from aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe
     from aiter import ActivationType, QuantType

     def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         (hidden_states, gate_up_weight, down_weight,
          gate_up_weight_scale, down_weight_scale,
          gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,
          gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled, down_weight_scale_shuffled,
          topk_weights, topk_ids, config) = data
         hidden_pad = config["d_hidden_pad"] - config["d_hidden"]
         intermediate_pad = config["d_expert_pad"] - config["d_expert"]
         num_experts = gate_up_weight_shuffled.shape[0]
         estimated_m = topk_ids.numel() // num_experts
         if estimated_m < 50:
             os.environ["AITER_KSPLIT"] = "2"
         else:
             os.environ.pop("AITER_KSPLIT", None)
         return fused_moe(hidden_states, gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,
             topk_weights, topk_ids, expert_mask=None,
             activation=ActivationType.Silu, quant_type=QuantType.per_1x32,
             doweight_stage1=False, w1_scale=gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
             w2_scale=down_weight_scale_shuffled, a1_scale=None, a2_scale=None,
             hidden_pad=hidden_pad, intermediate_pad=intermediate_pad)
     ```

   - **`kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py`** — Reverted to clean ref_kernel delegation (GEMM is closed)
     ```python
     """MXFP4 GEMM — ref_kernel delegation (ceiling).
     GEMM custom pipeline is fully closed: gemm_a4w4_asm has JIT callsite
     sensitivity that prevents ANY direct call from submission.py."""
     from task import input_t, output_t
     from reference import ref_kernel
     def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         return ref_kernel(data)
     ```

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission_introspect2.py`** — Focused introspection probe (submitted, results partially captured)
     - Filters only MoE-related CSVs (`fmoe` or `moe` in filename)
     - Dumps `get_2stage_cfgs` source, env var references, fused_moe dispatch lines, module exports
     - Results: Got tuned_fmoe.csv and some model_configs, but get_2stage_cfgs source and env var refs were STILL truncated

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission_online_tune.py`** — Created but NOT YET SUBMITTED
     ```python
     """MXFP4 MoE — Phase 7: Adaptive KSPLIT + AITER_ONLINE_TUNE=1."""
     import os
     from task import input_t, output_t
     from aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe
     from aiter import ActivationType, QuantType
     os.environ["AITER_ONLINE_TUNE"] = "1"
     def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         # Same adaptive KSPLIT logic as Phase 6 + AITER_ONLINE_TUNE=1
         ...
     ```

   - **`~/.claude/skills/aiter-kernel-parameter-semantics/SKILL.md`** — Updated to v1.4.0
     - Added full AITER_KSPLIT section: two-path switching, benchmark data table, adaptive pattern, JIT cost
     - Added Stderr Diagnostics section: how to read log lines to determine CSV-tuned vs default path

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission_ksplit4.py`** — Deleted (was temporary KSPLIT=4 test)
   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission_introspect.py`** — First introspection probe (too much GEMM CSV output)

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/reference.py`** — Read for understanding; shows ref_kernel calls `fused_moe` with shuffled weights, `ActivationType.Silu`, `QuantType.per_1x32`, `doweight_stage1=False`
   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/task.py`** — Read for understanding; defines input_t tuple with 12 elements including config dict

4. Errors and Fixes:
   - **Introspection output truncated by GEMM CSVs**: First probe (submission_introspect.py) dumped ALL CSV files. The GEMM CSVs (a4w4, a8w8) were 1000+ lines each and filled the stderr buffer before the key dispatch logic printed.
     - Fix: Created submission_introspect2.py that filters only files containing 'fmoe' or 'moe' in filename
   - **Second introspection STILL truncated**: tuned_fmoe.csv itself was large enough (with bfloat16 and Gelu configs for cu_num=80 and 256) that the get_2stage_cfgs source and env var references were cut off again.
     - Not yet fixed: Need a third probe that prints ONLY the source code sections, skipping CSV dumps entirely
   - **Popcorn CLI has no `leaderboard` subcommand**: Tried `popcorn-cli leaderboard` but it doesn't exist. Used `submissions list` and `submissions show` instead, but these don't show rank position or scores.
