# /learn - Online Learning System

**Extract reusable knowledge from this session into skills.** Evaluates what was learned, checks for existing skills, and creates new ones when valuable patterns are discovered.

---

## TABLE OF CONTENTS

| Phase       | Description                                            | Steps   |
| ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------ | ------- |
| **Phase 0** | Reference: triggers, quality criteria, skill structure | 0.1–0.4 |
| **Phase 1** | Evaluate: assess if knowledge is worth extracting      | 1.1     |
| **Phase 2** | Check Existing: search for related skills              | 2.1     |
| **Phase 3** | Create Skill: write the skill file                     | 3.1–3.2 |
| **Phase 4** | Quality Gates: final checklist before saving           | 4.1     |

**Quick Step Reference:**

- 0.1 Triggers, 0.2 Quality criteria, 0.3 What NOT to extract, 0.4 Skill structure
- 1.1 Self-evaluation questions
- 2.1 Search existing skills
- 3.1 Choose location, 3.2 Write SKILL.md
- 4.1 Final checklist

---

## PHASE 0: REFERENCE

### Step 0.1: Triggers

Invoke `/learn` after ANY task involving:

| Trigger                      | Example                                                          |
| ---------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Non-obvious debugging**    | Spent 10+ minutes investigating; solution wasn't in docs         |
| **Misleading errors**        | Error message pointed wrong direction; found real cause          |
| **Workarounds**              | Found limitation and creative solution                           |
| **Tool integration**         | Figured out how to use tool/API in undocumented way              |
| **Trial-and-error**          | Tried multiple approaches before finding what worked             |
| **Repeatable workflow**      | Multi-step task that will recur; worth standardizing             |
| **External service queries** | Fetched data from Jira, GitHub, Confluence, or other APIs        |
| **User-facing automation**   | Built something user will ask for again (reports, status checks) |

### Step 0.2: Quality Criteria

Skills must be:

- **Reusable**: Will help with future tasks (not just this instance)
- **Non-trivial**: Required discovery, OR is a valuable workflow pattern
- **Verified**: Solution actually worked, not theoretical

### Step 0.3: What NOT to Extract

- Single-step tasks with no workflow value
- One-off fixes unlikely to recur
- Knowledge easily found in official docs

### Step 0.4: Skill Structure

**Location:** `.claude/skills/[skill-name]/SKILL.md`

**Template:**

```markdown
---
name: descriptive-kebab-case-name
description: |
  [CRITICAL: This determines when the skill triggers. Include:]
  - What the skill does
  - Specific trigger conditions (exact error messages, symptoms)
  - When to use it (contexts, scenarios)
author: Claude Code
version: 1.0.0
---

# Skill Name

## Problem

[Clear description of the problem]

## Context / Trigger Conditions

[When to use - exact error messages, symptoms, scenarios]

## Solution

[Step-by-step solution]

## Verification

[How to verify it worked]

## Example

[Concrete example]

## References

[Links to documentation]
```

<details>
<summary>Writing Effective Descriptions</summary>

The description field is CRITICAL for skill discovery:

**Good:**

```yaml
description: |
  Fix for "ENOENT: no such file or directory" errors in npm monorepos.
  Use when: (1) npm run fails with ENOENT, (2) paths work in root but
  not in packages, (3) symlinked dependencies cause failures.
```

**Bad:**

```yaml
description: Helps with npm problems in monorepos.
```

</details>

**Guidelines:**

- **Concise** - Claude is smart; only add what it doesn't know
- **Under 500 lines** - Move large docs to `references/`
- **Examples over explanations** - Show, don't tell

---

## PHASE 1: EVALUATE

### Step 1.1: Self-Evaluation Questions

Ask yourself:

1. "What did I just learn that wasn't obvious before starting?"
2. "Would future-me benefit from having this documented?"
3. "Was the solution non-obvious from documentation alone?"
4. "Is this a multi-step workflow I'd repeat on similar tasks?"
5. "Did I query an external service the user will ask about again?"

**If NO to all → Skip extraction, nothing to learn.**

**Note:** External service queries (Jira tickets, GitHub PRs, Confluence pages) are almost always worth extracting - users frequently repeat these requests.

---

## PHASE 2: CHECK EXISTING

### Step 2.1: Search for Related Skills

Before creating, search for related skills:

```bash
ls .claude/skills/ 2>/dev/null
rg -i "keyword" .claude/skills/ 2>/dev/null
ls ~/.claude/pilot/skills/ 2>/dev/null
rg -i "keyword" ~/.claude/pilot/skills/ 2>/dev/null
```

| Found                | Action                           |
| -------------------- | -------------------------------- |
| Nothing related      | Create new skill                 |
| Same trigger and fix | Update existing (bump version)   |
| Partial overlap      | Update existing with new variant |

---

## PHASE 3: CREATE SKILL

### Step 3.1: Choose Location

**Project skills**: `.claude/skills/[skill-name]/SKILL.md`

### Step 3.2: Write SKILL.md

Use the template from Step 0.4. Ensure the description field contains specific trigger conditions.

---

## PHASE 4: QUALITY GATES

### Step 4.1: Final Checklist

Before finalizing:

- [ ] Description contains specific trigger conditions
- [ ] Solution verified to work
- [ ] Content specific enough to be actionable
- [ ] Content general enough to be reusable
- [ ] No sensitive information

---

## EXAMPLE

**Scenario**: Discovered LSP `findReferences` can find dead code by checking if functions have only 1 reference (their definition) or only test references.

**Skill Created**: `.claude/skills/lsp-dead-code-finder/SKILL.md`

```markdown
---
name: lsp-dead-code-finder
description: |
  Find dead/unused code using LSP findReferences. Use when: (1) user asks
  to find dead code, (2) cleaning up codebase, (3) refactoring. Key insight:
  function with only 1 reference (definition) or only test refs is dead code.
---

# LSP Dead Code Finder

...
```

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user is competing in the Luma AMD Speedrun competition, optimizing GPU kernels for AMD MI355X. The session continued from a compacted conversation. The user's directives were:
   - `/compact` — Triggered context compaction
   - `/learn` — Extract reusable knowledge from the session
   - The overarching goal (from prior context): "Well we obviously aren't at the top. I believe in you. Resume your plan." — Push MoE optimization to close the gap to the 145 µs leader.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG=1**: Forces the heuristic dispatch path even when pre-tuned CSV configs exist. This was the BREAKTHROUGH discovery — it unlocks the cktile path for 257-expert shapes that were locked to ksplit=0 by CSV configs.
   - **get_2stage_cfgs dispatch logic**: The core dispatch function in aiter's fused_moe. Uses `cfg_2stages` dict (loaded from CSV) keyed by `(cu_num, token, model_dim, inter_dim, expert, topk, activation, dtype, q_dtype_a, q_dtype_w, q_type, use_g1u1, doweight_stage1)`.
   - **cktile path trigger conditions**: Requires ALL of: `dtype in [bf16, fp16]`, `q_type == per_1x32`, `q_dtype_w == fp4x2`, `ksplit > 1`, `is_shuffled=True`.
   - **CSV hot-cache vs benchmark cold-cache**: CSV configs were tuned at hot L2 cache conditions. The actual competition benchmark clears L2 between runs. The cktile path (split_k=2) performs better at cold cache due to better K-dimension parallelism, explaining why bypassing CSV-tuned ksplit=0 is faster.
   - **Expert-count-aware KSPLIT**: KSPLIT=4 is better than KSPLIT=2 specifically for 257-expert shapes (dexp=256, small weights per expert) at estimated_m≈4, but NOT for 33-expert shapes (dexp=512+) with the same estimated_m.
   - **dsv3_fp4_tuned_fmoe.csv**: Contains MXFP4-specific (per_1x32) DeepSeek-V3 configs for 257-expert shapes with dhidden=7168, dexpert=256. All entries have ksplit=0.
   - **Benchmark shapes** (from task.yml): 7 shapes total — 3 TP=8 (257E, dexp=256), 3 TP=4 (33E, dexp=512), 1 EP (33E, dexp=2048). Ranking by geometric mean.
   - **Stderr buffer overflow pattern**: Large CSV files in aiter configs fill Popcorn's stderr buffer, truncating source code introspection output. Solution: use separate probes filtering only target files.

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission.py`** — Production MoE submission (updated to Phase 8)
     - Updated from Phase 6 (adaptive KSPLIT only) to Phase 8 (BYPASS_TUNE + adaptive KSPLIT)
     ```python
     """MXFP4 MoE — Phase 8: BYPASS_TUNE + Adaptive KSPLIT."""
     import os
     from task import input_t, output_t
     from aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe
     from aiter import ActivationType, QuantType

     def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         (hidden_states, gate_up_weight, down_weight,
          gate_up_weight_scale, down_weight_scale,
          gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,
          gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled, down_weight_scale_shuffled,
          topk_weights, topk_ids, config) = data
         hidden_pad = config["d_hidden_pad"] - config["d_hidden"]
         intermediate_pad = config["d_expert_pad"] - config["d_expert"]
         num_experts = gate_up_weight_shuffled.shape[0]
         estimated_m = topk_ids.numel() // num_experts
         if estimated_m < 50:
             os.environ["AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG"] = "1"
             os.environ["AITER_KSPLIT"] = "2"
         else:
             os.environ.pop("AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG", None)
             os.environ.pop("AITER_KSPLIT", None)
         return fused_moe(hidden_states, gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,
             topk_weights, topk_ids, expert_mask=None,
             activation=ActivationType.Silu, quant_type=QuantType.per_1x32,
             doweight_stage1=False, w1_scale=gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
             w2_scale=down_weight_scale_shuffled, a1_scale=None, a2_scale=None,
             hidden_pad=hidden_pad, intermediate_pad=intermediate_pad)
     ```

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission_srconly.py`** — Source-only introspection probe
     - Filters only `dsv3_fp4` or `fp4` CSVs, prints get_2stage_cfgs source, env var refs, dispatch lines
     - Successfully captured the full dispatch logic and CSV configs without buffer overflow

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission_bypass_tune.py`** — BYPASS_TUNE + KSPLIT=2 (the breakthrough submission)
     - Same logic as updated submission.py, submitted to leaderboard successfully at ~156 µs

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission_ksplit4.py`** — Tiered KSPLIT=4/2 strategy
     - AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG=1 always for sparse; KSPLIT=4 for estimated_m<10, KSPLIT=2 for 10-50
     - Results: Mixed — KSPLIT=4 better for bs=128, 257E (-7.5%) but worse for 33E shapes

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission_optimal.py`** — Expert-count-aware strategy
     - KSPLIT=4 for num_experts≥200 AND estimated_m<10; KSPLIT=2 for other sparse; KSPLIT=0 for dense
     - Benchmark results: 91.6, 171, 284, 59.8, 109, 214, 355 µs (geomean ≈ 155 µs, marginal improvement)
     ```python
     if estimated_m >= 50:
         os.environ.pop("AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG", None)
         os.environ.pop("AITER_KSPLIT", None)
     elif num_experts >= 200 and estimated_m < 10:
         os.environ["AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG"] = "1"
         os.environ["AITER_KSPLIT"] = "4"
     else:
         os.environ["AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG"] = "1"
         os.environ["AITER_KSPLIT"] = "2"
     ```

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/task.yml`** — Read to discover exact benchmark shapes

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission_online_tune.py`** — AITER_ONLINE_TUNE=1 test (created prior session, submitted this session)

   - `AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG=1` discovered as the key to overriding CSV-tuned ksplit=0 for 257-expert shapes. Combined with AITER_KSPLIT=2, this forces the cktile path which is dramatically faster at cold-cache benchmark conditions. Result: 40% improvement on bs=16 257E shape, overall geomean from 166 µs → 156 µs.

   **SOLVED — Introspection data capture:**

   - **Clean up temporary files**: submission_introspect.py, submission_introspect2.py, submission_srconly.py, submission_bypass_tune.py, submission_ksplit4.py, submission_optimal.py
   - **GEMM and MLA kernels**: Still at ceiling (ref_kernel delegation). Plan tasks 1-2 (GEMM) and 5 (MLA) from adaptive-baking-globe.md remain untouched.

8. Current Work:
   Immediately before the summary request, I had just received the benchmark results for `submission_optimal.py` (Phase 10: expert-count-aware KSPLIT strategy). The results showed:
   - bs=16, 257E: 91.6 µs (KSPLIT=4)

   - bs=16, 33E: 59.8 µs (KSPLIT=2)

   - bs=512, 33E(2048): 355 µs (KSPLIT=0)
   - Geomean ≈ 155 µs (marginal improvement over Phase 8's 156 µs)

   The current leaderboard submission is Phase 8 (`submission_bypass_tune.py`) at ~156 µs. The optimal strategy gives ~155 µs but hasn't been submitted to the leaderboard.

   **Current standings:**

The fused_moe dispatch auto-selects `block_m = 16 if token < 2048 else 32 if token < 16384 else 64` for cktile. Overriding block_m per shape might yield better results.

# /learn - Online Learning System

**Extract reusable knowledge from this session into skills.** Evaluates what was learned, checks for existing skills, and creates new ones when valuable patterns are discovered.

---

## TABLE OF CONTENTS

| Phase       | Description                                            | Steps   |
| ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------ | ------- |
| **Phase 0** | Reference: triggers, quality criteria, skill structure | 0.1–0.4 |
| **Phase 1** | Evaluate: assess if knowledge is worth extracting      | 1.1     |
| **Phase 2** | Check Existing: search for related skills              | 2.1     |
| **Phase 3** | Create Skill: write the skill file                     | 3.1–3.2 |
| **Phase 4** | Quality Gates: final checklist before saving           | 4.1     |

**Quick Step Reference:**

- 0.1 Triggers, 0.2 Quality criteria, 0.3 What NOT to extract, 0.4 Skill structure
- 1.1 Self-evaluation questions
- 2.1 Search existing skills
- 3.1 Choose location, 3.2 Write SKILL.md
- 4.1 Final checklist

---

## PHASE 0: REFERENCE

### Step 0.1: Triggers

Invoke `/learn` after ANY task involving:

| Trigger                      | Example                                                          |
| ---------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Non-obvious debugging**    | Spent 10+ minutes investigating; solution wasn't in docs         |
| **Misleading errors**        | Error message pointed wrong direction; found real cause          |
| **Workarounds**              | Found limitation and creative solution                           |
| **Tool integration**         | Figured out how to use tool/API in undocumented way              |
| **Trial-and-error**          | Tried multiple approaches before finding what worked             |
| **Repeatable workflow**      | Multi-step task that will recur; worth standardizing             |
| **External service queries** | Fetched data from Jira, GitHub, Confluence, or other APIs        |
| **User-facing automation**   | Built something user will ask for again (reports, status checks) |

### Step 0.2: Quality Criteria

Skills must be:

- **Reusable**: Will help with future tasks (not just this instance)
- **Non-trivial**: Required discovery, OR is a valuable workflow pattern
- **Verified**: Solution actually worked, not theoretical

### Step 0.3: What NOT to Extract

- Single-step tasks with no workflow value
- One-off fixes unlikely to recur
- Knowledge easily found in official docs

### Step 0.4: Skill Structure

**Location:** `.claude/skills/[skill-name]/SKILL.md`

**Template:**

```markdown
---
name: descriptive-kebab-case-name
description: |
  [CRITICAL: This determines when the skill triggers. Include:]
  - What the skill does
  - Specific trigger conditions (exact error messages, symptoms)
  - When to use it (contexts, scenarios)
author: Claude Code
version: 1.0.0
---

# Skill Name

## Problem

[Clear description of the problem]

## Context / Trigger Conditions

[When to use - exact error messages, symptoms, scenarios]

## Solution

[Step-by-step solution]

## Verification

[How to verify it worked]

## Example

[Concrete example]

## References

[Links to documentation]
```

<details>
<summary>Writing Effective Descriptions</summary>

The description field is CRITICAL for skill discovery:

**Good:**

```yaml
description: |
  Fix for "ENOENT: no such file or directory" errors in npm monorepos.
  Use when: (1) npm run fails with ENOENT, (2) paths work in root but
  not in packages, (3) symlinked dependencies cause failures.
```

**Bad:**

```yaml
description: Helps with npm problems in monorepos.
```

</details>

**Guidelines:**

- **Concise** - Claude is smart; only add what it doesn't know
- **Under 500 lines** - Move large docs to `references/`
- **Examples over explanations** - Show, don't tell

---

## PHASE 1: EVALUATE

### Step 1.1: Self-Evaluation Questions

Ask yourself:

1. "What did I just learn that wasn't obvious before starting?"
2. "Would future-me benefit from having this documented?"
3. "Was the solution non-obvious from documentation alone?"
4. "Is this a multi-step workflow I'd repeat on similar tasks?"
5. "Did I query an external service the user will ask about again?"

**If NO to all → Skip extraction, nothing to learn.**

**Note:** External service queries (Jira tickets, GitHub PRs, Confluence pages) are almost always worth extracting - users frequently repeat these requests.

---

## PHASE 2: CHECK EXISTING

### Step 2.1: Search for Related Skills

Before creating, search for related skills:

```bash
ls .claude/skills/ 2>/dev/null
rg -i "keyword" .claude/skills/ 2>/dev/null
ls ~/.claude/pilot/skills/ 2>/dev/null
rg -i "keyword" ~/.claude/pilot/skills/ 2>/dev/null
```

| Found                | Action                           |
| -------------------- | -------------------------------- |
| Nothing related      | Create new skill                 |
| Same trigger and fix | Update existing (bump version)   |
| Partial overlap      | Update existing with new variant |

---

## PHASE 3: CREATE SKILL

### Step 3.1: Choose Location

**Project skills**: `.claude/skills/[skill-name]/SKILL.md`

### Step 3.2: Write SKILL.md

Use the template from Step 0.4. Ensure the description field contains specific trigger conditions.

---

## PHASE 4: QUALITY GATES

### Step 4.1: Final Checklist

Before finalizing:

- [ ] Description contains specific trigger conditions
- [ ] Solution verified to work
- [ ] Content specific enough to be actionable
- [ ] Content general enough to be reusable
- [ ] No sensitive information

---

## EXAMPLE

**Scenario**: Discovered LSP `findReferences` can find dead code by checking if functions have only 1 reference (their definition) or only test references.

**Skill Created**: `.claude/skills/lsp-dead-code-finder/SKILL.md`

```markdown
---
name: lsp-dead-code-finder
description: |
  Find dead/unused code using LSP findReferences. Use when: (1) user asks
  to find dead code, (2) cleaning up codebase, (3) refactoring. Key insight:
  function with only 1 reference (definition) or only test refs is dead code.
---

# LSP Dead Code Finder

...
```

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user is competing in the Luma AMD Speedrun competition, optimizing GPU kernels (MoE, GEMM, MLA) for AMD MI355X. The overarching directive from prior context was "Resume your plan" to push optimization further. In this session:
   - `/compact` — Triggered context compaction
   - `/learn` — Extract reusable knowledge from session
   - "Continue" — Resume optimization work after learning extraction
   - User provided the full MoE competition description and reference implementation as additional context
   - The conversation culminates with a shift from MoE (at API ceiling) to GEMM optimization

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **AITER_KSPLIT values 2-4 are the safe/useful range** — KSPLIT=3 ties Phase 10, KSPLIT=8 causes correctness failures
   - **fused_moe_ is a JIT-compiled C++ operator** (torch.ops.aiter.fused_moe_), NOT a Python function — direct kernel calls from Python are blocked
   - **block_size_M parameter is IGNORED when BYPASS_TUNE is on** — the heuristic computes its own block_m internally
   - **Popcorn CLI requires file to be named `submission.py`** — uploading files with other names causes "File not found" server error
   - **gemm_a4w4_asm JIT callsite sensitivity** — calling gemm_a4w4 from submission.py produces wrong results; calling through library wrappers (e.g., fused_moe.py) works
   - **cktile_moe_gemm1/gemm2 function signatures** discovered from JIT build logs
   - **ck_moe_stage1/stage2 function signatures** also captured from JIT build logs
   - **MoE optimization ceiling** reached at ~154.5 µs geomean (6.6% gap to 145 µs leader)
   - **GEMM alternatives**: `get_torch_quant` (pure PyTorch, no Triton JIT), `gemm_afp4wfp4` (Triton GEMM, different from ASM path), `get_hip_quant`

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`~/.claude/skills/aiter-kernel-parameter-semantics/SKILL.md`** — Updated from v1.5.0 to v1.6.0
     - Added expert-count-aware KSPLIT rule (3-tier: K4 for 257E sparse, K2 for moderate sparse, K0 for dense)
     - Added 257E benchmark data with BYPASS_TUNE
     - Added full 7-shape competition benchmark table
     - Added mechanistic explanation for why KSPLIT=4 helps 257E but not 33E
     - Updated adaptive KSPLIT code pattern to include expert-count awareness

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission.py`** — Currently Phase 10 optimal (restored after experiments)
     ```python
     """MXFP4 MoE — Phase 10: Expert-count-aware KSPLIT selection."""
     import os
     from task import input_t, output_t
     from aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe
     from aiter import ActivationType, QuantType

     def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         # ... unpack data tuple ...
         hidden_pad = config["d_hidden_pad"] - config["d_hidden"]
         intermediate_pad = config["d_expert_pad"] - config["d_expert"]
         num_experts = gate_up_weight_shuffled.shape[0]
         estimated_m = topk_ids.numel() // num_experts
         if estimated_m >= 50:
             os.environ.pop("AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG", None)
             os.environ.pop("AITER_KSPLIT", None)
         elif num_experts >= 200 and estimated_m < 10:
             os.environ["AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG"] = "1"
             os.environ["AITER_KSPLIT"] = "4"
         else:
             os.environ["AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG"] = "1"
             os.environ["AITER_KSPLIT"] = "2"
         return fused_moe(hidden_states, gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,
             topk_weights, topk_ids, expert_mask=None, activation=ActivationType.Silu,
             quant_type=QuantType.per_1x32, doweight_stage1=False,
             w1_scale=gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled, w2_scale=down_weight_scale_shuffled,
             a1_scale=None, a2_scale=None, hidden_pad=hidden_pad, intermediate_pad=intermediate_pad)
     ```
     - This is the leaderboard version at ~154.5 µs geomean

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission_ksplit3.py`** — KSPLIT=3 test for 257E sparse
     - Used KSPLIT=3 for num_experts>=200 AND estimated_m<10
     - Result: geomean 155.5 µs (tied with Phase 10, KSPLIT=3 between K2 and K4 for 257E)

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission_ksplit8.py`** — KSPLIT=8 test
     - FAILED correctness on bs=16, 257E; slower on bs=128, 257E (183 µs vs 171 µs)
     - Conclusion: KSPLIT=8 is both incorrect and slower — ruled out

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission_blockm.py`** — block_size_M=32 override test
     - Discovered block_size_M is IGNORED when BYPASS_TUNE is on
     - Stderr confirmed heuristic's block_m (64) was used, not the requested 32

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission_direct_cktile.py`** — fused_moe full source probe
     - Captured `fused_moe()` is a thin wrapper around `fused_moe_()`
     - `splitk=0` parameter in fused_moe is NOT forwarded to fused_moe_
     - Discovery: fused_moe() passes all params except splitk

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission_fmoe_inner.py`** — fused_moe_ inner probe
     - Revealed fused_moe_ is a JIT C++ wrapper (only 399 chars)
     - Actual logic in torch.ops.aiter C++ operator

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission_cktile_probe.py`** — cktile function signature probe

   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission_phase8_backup.py`** — Backup of Phase 8

   - **`kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py`** — Currently has GEMM probe code (submission_probe.py content)
     ```python
     """MXFP4 GEMM — Probe: Discover available quantization and GEMM alternatives."""
     # Tests get_torch_quant, get_hip_quant, gemm_afp4wfp4, other GEMM functions
     # Delegates to ref_kernel for correctness
     ```

   - **`kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission_probe.py`** — GEMM alternatives probe
     - Tests: get_torch_quant shuffle=True, get_hip_quant, gemm_afp4wfp4 existence/signature, dynamic_mxfp4_quant
     - Zero-risk (delegates to ref_kernel)

   - **`kernels/mxfp4-mm/reference.py`** — GEMM reference implementation
     - Uses `get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)` with `shuffle=True` for A quantization
     - Calls `aiter.gemm_a4w4(A_q, B_shuffle, A_scale_sh, B_scale_sh, dtype=dtypes.bf16, bpreshuffle=True)`
     - Mentions `gemm_afp4wfp4` Triton alternative at line 97
     - Reference times: 8-21 µs across shapes, leader at 9.7 µs

   - **Kernel function signatures discovered from JIT logs:**
     ```python
     cktile_moe_gemm1(XQ, WQ, Y, sorted_ids, sorted_expert_ids, max_token_ids, topk,
                      n_padded_zeros=0, k_padded_zeros=0, topk_weight=None,
                      x_scale=None, w_scale=None, exp_bias=None,
                      activation=0, block_m=32, split_k=1) -> Tensor

     cktile_moe_gemm2(XQ, WQ, Y, sorted_ids, sorted_expert_ids, max_token_ids, topk,
                      n_padded_zeros=0, k_padded_zeros=0, topk_weight=None,
                      x_scale=None, w_scale=None, exp_bias=None,
                      activation=0, block_m=32, split_k=1) -> Tensor

     ck_moe_stage1(hidden_states, w1, w2, sorted_token_ids, sorted_expert_ids,
                   num_valid_ids, out, topk, kernelName=None, w1_scale=None, a1_scale=None,
                   block_m=32, sorted_weights=None, quant_type=0, activation=0,
                   splitk=1, non_temporal_load=False, dst_type=None, is_shuffled=True)

     ck_moe_stage2(inter_states, w1, w2, sorted_token_ids, sorted_expert_ids,
                   num_valid_ids, out, topk, kernelName=None, w2_scale=None, a2_scale=None,
                   block_m=32, sorted_weights=None, quant_type=0, activation=0,
                   splitk=1, non_temporal_load=False, dst_type=None, is_shuffled=True)

     moe_sorting_fwd(topk_ids, topk_weights, sorted_token_ids, sorted_weights,
                     sorted_expert_ids, num_valid_ids, moe_buf, num_experts, unit_size,
                     local_expert_mask=None, num_local_tokens=None, dispatch_policy=0)
     ```

# /learn - Online Learning System

**Extract reusable knowledge from this session into skills.** Evaluates what was learned, checks for existing skills, and creates new ones when valuable patterns are discovered.

---

## TABLE OF CONTENTS

| Phase       | Description                                            | Steps   |
| ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------ | ------- |
| **Phase 0** | Reference: triggers, quality criteria, skill structure | 0.1–0.4 |
| **Phase 1** | Evaluate: assess if knowledge is worth extracting      | 1.1     |
| **Phase 2** | Check Existing: search for related skills              | 2.1     |
| **Phase 3** | Create Skill: write the skill file                     | 3.1–3.2 |
| **Phase 4** | Quality Gates: final checklist before saving           | 4.1     |

**Quick Step Reference:**

- 0.1 Triggers, 0.2 Quality criteria, 0.3 What NOT to extract, 0.4 Skill structure
- 1.1 Self-evaluation questions
- 2.1 Search existing skills
- 3.1 Choose location, 3.2 Write SKILL.md
- 4.1 Final checklist

---

## PHASE 0: REFERENCE

### Step 0.1: Triggers

Invoke `/learn` after ANY task involving:

| Trigger                      | Example                                                          |
| ---------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Non-obvious debugging**    | Spent 10+ minutes investigating; solution wasn't in docs         |
| **Misleading errors**        | Error message pointed wrong direction; found real cause          |
| **Workarounds**              | Found limitation and creative solution                           |
| **Tool integration**         | Figured out how to use tool/API in undocumented way              |
| **Trial-and-error**          | Tried multiple approaches before finding what worked             |
| **Repeatable workflow**      | Multi-step task that will recur; worth standardizing             |
| **External service queries** | Fetched data from Jira, GitHub, Confluence, or other APIs        |
| **User-facing automation**   | Built something user will ask for again (reports, status checks) |

### Step 0.2: Quality Criteria

Skills must be:

- **Reusable**: Will help with future tasks (not just this instance)
- **Non-trivial**: Required discovery, OR is a valuable workflow pattern
- **Verified**: Solution actually worked, not theoretical

### Step 0.3: What NOT to Extract

- Single-step tasks with no workflow value
- One-off fixes unlikely to recur
- Knowledge easily found in official docs

### Step 0.4: Skill Structure

**Location:** `.claude/skills/[skill-name]/SKILL.md`

**Template:**

```markdown
---
name: descriptive-kebab-case-name
description: |
  [CRITICAL: This determines when the skill triggers. Include:]
  - What the skill does
  - Specific trigger conditions (exact error messages, symptoms)
  - When to use it (contexts, scenarios)
author: Claude Code
version: 1.0.0
---

# Skill Name

## Problem

[Clear description of the problem]

## Context / Trigger Conditions

[When to use - exact error messages, symptoms, scenarios]

## Solution

[Step-by-step solution]

## Verification

[How to verify it worked]

## Example

[Concrete example]

## References

[Links to documentation]
```

<details>
<summary>Writing Effective Descriptions</summary>

The description field is CRITICAL for skill discovery:

**Good:**

```yaml
description: |
  Fix for "ENOENT: no such file or directory" errors in npm monorepos.
  Use when: (1) npm run fails with ENOENT, (2) paths work in root but
  not in packages, (3) symlinked dependencies cause failures.
```

**Bad:**

```yaml
description: Helps with npm problems in monorepos.
```

</details>

**Guidelines:**

- **Concise** - Claude is smart; only add what it doesn't know
- **Under 500 lines** - Move large docs to `references/`
- **Examples over explanations** - Show, don't tell

---

## PHASE 1: EVALUATE

### Step 1.1: Self-Evaluation Questions

Ask yourself:

1. "What did I just learn that wasn't obvious before starting?"
2. "Would future-me benefit from having this documented?"
3. "Was the solution non-obvious from documentation alone?"
4. "Is this a multi-step workflow I'd repeat on similar tasks?"
5. "Did I query an external service the user will ask about again?"

**If NO to all → Skip extraction, nothing to learn.**

**Note:** External service queries (Jira tickets, GitHub PRs, Confluence pages) are almost always worth extracting - users frequently repeat these requests.

---

## PHASE 2: CHECK EXISTING

### Step 2.1: Search for Related Skills

Before creating, search for related skills:

```bash
ls .claude/skills/ 2>/dev/null
rg -i "keyword" .claude/skills/ 2>/dev/null
ls ~/.claude/pilot/skills/ 2>/dev/null
rg -i "keyword" ~/.claude/pilot/skills/ 2>/dev/null
```

| Found                | Action                           |
| -------------------- | -------------------------------- |
| Nothing related      | Create new skill                 |
| Same trigger and fix | Update existing (bump version)   |
| Partial overlap      | Update existing with new variant |

---

## PHASE 3: CREATE SKILL

### Step 3.1: Choose Location

**Project skills**: `.claude/skills/[skill-name]/SKILL.md`

### Step 3.2: Write SKILL.md

Use the template from Step 0.4. Ensure the description field contains specific trigger conditions.

---

## PHASE 4: QUALITY GATES

### Step 4.1: Final Checklist

Before finalizing:

- [ ] Description contains specific trigger conditions
- [ ] Solution verified to work
- [ ] Content specific enough to be actionable
- [ ] Content general enough to be reusable
- [ ] No sensitive information

---

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user is competing in the Luma AMD Speedrun competition, optimizing GPU kernels for AMD MI355X. The overarching directive was "Continue" (resume the plan from prior sessions). The prior session concluded MoE optimization at API ceiling (~154.5 µs) and shifted focus to GEMM optimization (reference ~24 µs vs leader 9.7 µs, 2.49x gap). This session focused entirely on Task #18: implementing a custom GEMM kernel that bypasses the `gemm_a4w4_asm` JIT callsite sensitivity bug. Seven distinct approaches were attempted, all failed, and the GEMM kernel was restored to `ref_kernel` delegation.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **JIT callsite sensitivity**: `gemm_a4w4_asm` (ROCm HIP code object dispatcher) produces wrong results when called from `submission.py` context, regardless of the quantization function used
   - **Scale shuffle format incompatibility**: `get_torch_quant` and `get_hip_quant` produce different scale shuffle formats than `get_triton_quant` — only `get_triton_quant` format is compatible with `gemm_a4w4(bpreshuffle=True)`
   - **Triton type system limitations**: Custom ROCm dtypes (`REDACTED`, `REDACTED`) are not in Triton's `type_canonicalisation_dict`, blocking `gemm_afp4wfp4` use
   - **Popcorn CLI uploads only `submission.py`**: NOT the entire directory — multi-file submissions require dynamic module creation
   - **Dynamic module callsite bypass**: Creating modules at `/tmp/` via `importlib.util.spec_from_file_location` does NOT bypass the sensitivity — the dispatch mechanism is deeper than Python frame filename
   - **Pre-compiled HSA kernels**: `f4gemm_bf16_per1x32Fp4_BpreShuffle_32x128.co` and `f4gemm_bf16_per1x32Fp4_BpreShuffle_192x128.co` loaded via `hipModuleLoad`
   - **gemm_a4w4_asm signature**: `(A, B, A_scale, B_scale, out, kernelName, bias=None, alpha=1.0, beta=0.0, bpreshuffle=True, log2_k_split=None)`
   - **Benchmark shapes**: k=7168/m=8/n=2112, k=1536/m=16/n=3072, k=1536/m=64/n=3072, k=512/m=256/n=2880
   - M=256, N=2880, K=512 has NO tuned config in CKGEMM or asmGEMM

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py`** (FINAL — restored to ref_kernel delegation)
     - Modified 7+ times during this session, finally restored to simple delegation
     ```python
     """MXFP4 GEMM — At API ceiling: delegate to ref_kernel."""
     from task import input_t, output_t
     from reference import ref_kernel

     def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         return ref_kernel(data)
     ```

   - **`kernels/mxfp4-mm/reference.py`** (read, not modified)
     - Contains the reference implementation using `get_triton_quant` + `gemm_a4w4`
     - Key: calls `B = B.contiguous()` (redundant) and creates `quant_func` every call
     ```python
     def ref_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
         A = A.contiguous()
         B = B.contiguous()
         quant_func = aiter.get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
         A_q, A_scale_sh = quant_func(A, shuffle=True)
         out_gemm = aiter.gemm_a4w4(A_q, B_shuffle, A_scale_sh, B_scale_sh,
                                     dtype=dtypes.bf16, bpreshuffle=True)
         return out_gemm
     ```

   - **`kernels/mxfp4-mm/gemm_wrapper.py`** (created, not used — Popcorn doesn't upload it)
     ```python
     import aiter
     from aiter import QuantType, dtypes
     _quant_func = None
     def quant_and_gemm(A, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh):
         global _quant_func
         if _quant_func is None:
             _quant_func = aiter.get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
         A_q, A_scale_sh = _quant_func(A, shuffle=True)
         return aiter.gemm_a4w4(A_q, B_shuffle, A_scale_sh, B_scale_sh,
                                dtype=dtypes.bf16, bpreshuffle=True)
     ```

   - **`kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission_torch_quant.py`** (created, tested, failed)
     - Fallback variant using `get_torch_quant` instead of `get_triton_quant`

   - **`kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission_ksplit_probe.py`** (created, not submitted)
     - K-split timing probe for `log2_k_split` optimization

   - **`kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission_wrapper.py`** / **`submission_wrapper_bak.py`** (created, tested)
     - Wrapper approach that imports from `gemm_wrapper.py`

   - **`~/.claude/skills/amd-triton-jit-callsite-correctness/SKILL.md`** (read, not modified yet)
     - Documents the callsite sensitivity bug, v1.3.0
     - Key sections: "Confirmed: get_hip_quant Also Fails", "JIT Cache Warmup Does NOT Fix It"

   - **GEMM probe results** (`/tmp/claude-1000/-home-mike-anderson-dev-cohezion/tasks/bp1b3ywcv.output`)
     - Discovered available quantization and GEMM alternatives on MI355X
     - Key findings: `get_torch_quant(shuffle=True)` works, `gemm_afp4wfp4` exists, `get_hip_quant(shuffle=True)` works

4. Errors and Fixes:

   - **get_hip_quant + gemm_a4w4 correctness failure** (Task bkjqnne3l):
     - 14,144 mismatched elements, ERROR at (0,0): 165.0 vs 163.0
     - Root cause: `get_hip_quant` uses `dynamic_per_group_scaled_quant_fp4` which shuffles scales differently than `get_triton_quant`
     - Fix: Tried `get_torch_quant` instead (same result)

   - **get_torch_quant + gemm_a4w4 correctness failure** (Task bbyoo7f5o):
     - Identical errors to get_hip_quant (165.0 vs 163.0, same mismatch count)
     - Root cause: `gemm_a4w4_asm` callsite sensitivity — the GEMM dispatch is wrong when called from submission.py, regardless of quant function
     - Fix: Tried alternative GEMM path

   - **gemm_afp4wfp4 KeyError 'REDACTED'** (Task bmicdh5e7):
     - Triton's `type_canonicalisation_dict` doesn't include custom ROCm dtypes
     - Fix: Added `.view(torch.uint8)` for data tensors

   - **gemm_afp4wfp4 KeyError 'REDACTED'** (Task b9seojn0j):
     - Scale tensor also has custom dtype not in Triton's type dict
     - Fix: Added `.view(torch.uint8)` for ALL custom dtype tensors

   - **gemm_afp4wfp4 uint8 views wrong results** (Task bsrkgjjut):
     - 16,596 mismatched elements, ERROR at (0,0): 200.0 vs 163.0
     - LARGER errors than callsite bug — uint8 byte interpretation differs from what Triton kernel expects
     - Fix: Abandoned gemm_afp4wfp4 approach

   - **Wrapper module ModuleNotFoundError** (Task byea1ptug):
     - `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'gemm_wrapper'`
     - Root cause: Popcorn CLI only uploads `submission.py`, not additional files
     - Fix: Created dynamic module at `/tmp/` using `importlib.util.spec_from_file_location`

   - **Dynamic module still fails** (Task b3ek3snd7):
     - 13,996 mismatched elements, ERROR at (0,0): 166.0 vs 163.0
     - Different error value (166) than previous attempts (165) — confirms dispatch isn't filename-based
     - Fix: Accepted GEMM at API ceiling, restored ref_kernel delegation

5. Problem Solving:

   **SOLVED — GEMM probe analysis** (Task #17 from prior session):
   - Successfully read and analyzed probe results showing available quantization/GEMM functions on MI355X
   - Discovered `get_torch_quant`, `get_hip_quant`, `gemm_afp4wfp4`, and `log2_k_split` parameter

   **CONCLUDED — GEMM bypass attempts** (Task #18):
   - Exhaustively tested 7 approaches to bypass `gemm_a4w4_asm` callsite sensitivity:
     1. `get_hip_quant` + `gemm_a4w4` → wrong results (scale shuffle incompatibility)
     2. `get_torch_quant` + `gemm_a4w4` → wrong results (same issue)
     3. `gemm_afp4wfp4` with native dtypes → Triton dtype KeyError
     4. `gemm_afp4wfp4` with uint8 data views → Triton dtype KeyError (scales)
     5. `gemm_afp4wfp4` with all uint8 views → wrong results (data interpretation mismatch)
     6. Wrapper module (`gemm_wrapper.py`) → not uploaded by Popcorn CLI
     7. Dynamic module at `/tmp/` → still fails (dispatch not filename-based)
   - Conclusion: GEMM at API ceiling, only `ref_kernel` delegation produces correct results
   - GEMM submission.py restored to `ref_kernel` delegation

   **Key discovery**: The callsite sensitivity mechanism is NOT based on Python frame `__file__` attribute. # /learn - Online Learning System

**Extract reusable knowledge from this session into skills.** Evaluates what was learned, checks for existing skills, and creates new ones when valuable patterns are discovered.

---

## TABLE OF CONTENTS

| Phase       | Description                                            | Steps   |
| ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------ | ------- |
| **Phase 0** | Reference: triggers, quality criteria, skill structure | 0.1–0.4 |
| **Phase 1** | Evaluate: assess if knowledge is worth extracting      | 1.1     |
| **Phase 2** | Check Existing: search for related skills              | 2.1     |
| **Phase 3** | Create Skill: write the skill file                     | 3.1–3.2 |
| **Phase 4** | Quality Gates: final checklist before saving           | 4.1     |

**Quick Step Reference:**

- 0.1 Triggers, 0.2 Quality criteria, 0.3 What NOT to extract, 0.4 Skill structure
- 1.1 Self-evaluation questions
- 2.1 Search existing skills
- 3.1 Choose location, 3.2 Write SKILL.md
- 4.1 Final checklist

---

## PHASE 0: REFERENCE

### Step 0.1: Triggers

Invoke `/learn` after ANY task involving:

| Trigger                      | Example                                                          |
| ---------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Non-obvious debugging**    | Spent 10+ minutes investigating; solution wasn't in docs         |
| **Misleading errors**        | Error message pointed wrong direction; found real cause          |
| **Workarounds**              | Found limitation and creative solution                           |
| **Tool integration**         | Figured out how to use tool/API in undocumented way              |
| **Trial-and-error**          | Tried multiple approaches before finding what worked             |
| **Repeatable workflow**      | Multi-step task that will recur; worth standardizing             |
| **External service queries** | Fetched data from Jira, GitHub, Confluence, or other APIs        |
| **User-facing automation**   | Built something user will ask for again (reports, status checks) |

### Step 0.2: Quality Criteria

Skills must be:

- **Reusable**: Will help with future tasks (not just this instance)
- **Non-trivial**: Required discovery, OR is a valuable workflow pattern
- **Verified**: Solution actually worked, not theoretical

### Step 0.3: What NOT to Extract

- Single-step tasks with no workflow value
- One-off fixes unlikely to recur
- Knowledge easily found in official docs

### Step 0.4: Skill Structure

**Location:** `.claude/skills/[skill-name]/SKILL.md`

**Template:**

```markdown
---
name: descriptive-kebab-case-name
description: |
  [CRITICAL: This determines when the skill triggers. Include:]
  - What the skill does
  - Specific trigger conditions (exact error messages, symptoms)
  - When to use it (contexts, scenarios)
author: Claude Code
version: 1.0.0
---

# Skill Name

## Problem

[Clear description of the problem]

## Context / Trigger Conditions

[When to use - exact error messages, symptoms, scenarios]

## Solution

[Step-by-step solution]

## Verification

[How to verify it worked]

## Example

[Concrete example]

## References

[Links to documentation]
```

<details>
<summary>Writing Effective Descriptions</summary>

The description field is CRITICAL for skill discovery:

**Good:**

```yaml
description: |
  Fix for "ENOENT: no such file or directory" errors in npm monorepos.
  Use when: (1) npm run fails with ENOENT, (2) paths work in root but
  not in packages, (3) symlinked dependencies cause failures.
```

**Bad:**

```yaml
description: Helps with npm problems in monorepos.
```

</details>

**Guidelines:**

- **Concise** - Claude is smart; only add what it doesn't know
- **Under 500 lines** - Move large docs to `references/`
- **Examples over explanations** - Show, don't tell

---

## PHASE 1: EVALUATE

### Step 1.1: Self-Evaluation Questions

Ask yourself:

1. "What did I just learn that wasn't obvious before starting?"
2. "Would future-me benefit from having this documented?"
3. "Was the solution non-obvious from documentation alone?"
4. "Is this a multi-step workflow I'd repeat on similar tasks?"
5. "Did I query an external service the user will ask about again?"

**If NO to all → Skip extraction, nothing to learn.**

**Note:** External service queries (Jira tickets, GitHub PRs, Confluence pages) are almost always worth extracting - users frequently repeat these requests.

---

## PHASE 2: CHECK EXISTING

### Step 2.1: Search for Related Skills

Before creating, search for related skills:

```bash
ls .claude/skills/ 2>/dev/null
rg -i "keyword" .claude/skills/ 2>/dev/null
ls ~/.claude/pilot/skills/ 2>/dev/null
rg -i "keyword" ~/.claude/pilot/skills/ 2>/dev/null
```

| Found                | Action                           |
| -------------------- | -------------------------------- |
| Nothing related      | Create new skill                 |
| Same trigger and fix | Update existing (bump version)   |
| Partial overlap      | Update existing with new variant |

---

## PHASE 3: CREATE SKILL

### Step 3.1: Choose Location

**Project skills**: `.claude/skills/[skill-name]/SKILL.md`

### Step 3.2: Write SKILL.md

Use the template from Step 0.4. Ensure the description field contains specific trigger conditions.

---

## PHASE 4: QUALITY GATES

### Step 4.1: Final Checklist

Before finalizing:

- [ ] Description contains specific trigger conditions
- [ ] Solution verified to work
- [ ] Content specific enough to be actionable
- [ ] Content general enough to be reusable
- [ ] No sensitive information

Incorporate this context: """amd-mxfp4-mm
Deadline

Ends 2026-03-30 06:00 UTC
GPU types

MI355X
Description
You will implement a quantize func and block scaled MXFP4 matrix-matrix multiplication kernel optimized for AMD Instinct MI355X GPU. To be explicit, you will be given a tuple of tensors:

(A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh)
where:

A is M x K in K-major order in bfloat16
B is N x K in K-major order in bfloat16
B_q is N x K/2 in K-major order in MXFP4
B_shuffle is N x K/2 in shuffled order in MXFP4, shuffled to (16,16) tile coalesced
B_scale_sh is * x K/32 in E8M0, * means it will be padded.
Then, the kernel flow is bf16 A, MXFP4 B -> MXFP4 per-1x32 quant A -> gemm_a4w4 -> BF16 C [m,n]. To be specific, the invocation flow is: (1) Quant A to MXFP4: aiter.get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32). (2) GEMM: aiter.gemm_a4w4. m, n divisible by 64; k divisible by 64.

The ranking criteria is the geometric mean of the benchmark results. Pls note that this is the elimination round, whoever rank top5 are selected into the next round, e2e optimization for deepseek-R1-MXFP4 and GPTOSS-MXFP4 mdoel

The aiter performance is:
M   N    K   time[us]
  4 2880   512  8.198
 16 2112  7168 20.873
 32 4096   512  9.462
 32 2880   512  9.173
 64 7168  2048 12.738
256 3072  1536 12.219
Benchmark Shapes
{"k":512,"m":4,"n":2880}
{"k":7168,"m":16,"n":2112}
{"k":512,"m":32,"n":4096}
{"k":512,"m":32,"n":2880}
{"k":2048,"m":64,"n":7168}
{"k":1536,"m":256,"n":3072}""" and this reference implementation: """
FP4 quant + FP4 GEMM reference: bf16 A, MXFP4 B -> MXFP4 per-1x32 quant A -> gemm_a4w4 -> bf16 C.
Quant logic follows aiter op_tests/test_gemm_a4w4.py (get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)).

NOTE: Explicitly uses dynamic_mxfp4_quant from aiter.ops.triton.quant (patched in #975)
      rather than going through aiter.get_triton_quant, which may dispatch to the
      unpatched fp4_utils.py kernel. See ROCm/aiter#974, ROCm/aiter#975.
"""
import torch
from task import input_t, output_t
from utils import make_match_reference
from aiter import QuantType,dtypes
import aiter
from aiter.ops.shuffle import shuffle_weight
from aiter.ops.triton.quant import dynamic_mxfp4_quant  # #975-patched kernel
from aiter.utility.fp4_utils import e8m0_shuffle
# K must be divisible by 64 (scale group 32 and fp4 pack 2)
SCALE_GROUP_SIZE = 32

def _quant_mxfp4(x, shuffle=True):
    x_fp4, bs_e8m0 = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(x)
    if shuffle:
        bs_e8m0 = e8m0_shuffle(bs_e8m0)
    return x_fp4.view(dtypes.fp4x2), bs_e8m0.view(dtypes.fp8_e8m0)

def generate_input(m: int, n: int, k: int, seed: int):# -> input_t:
    """
    Generate random bf16 inputs A [m, k], B [n, k] and quantized MXFP4 B, shuffled B and B_scale.

    Returns:
        Tuple of (A, B), both bf16 on cuda.
    """
    assert k % 64 == 0, "k must be divisible by 64 (scale group 32 and fp4 pack 2)"
    gen = torch.Generator(device="cuda")
    gen.manual_seed(seed)
    A = torch.randn((m, k), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda", generator=gen)
    B = torch.randn((n, k), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda", generator=gen)
    B_q, B_scale_sh = _quant_mxfp4(B, shuffle=True)
    # shuffle B(weight) to (16,16) tile coalesced
    B_shuffle = shuffle_weight(B_q, layout=(16, 16))
    return (A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh)

def run_torch_fp4_mm(
    x: torch.Tensor,
    w: torch.Tensor,
    x_scales: torch.Tensor,
    w_scales: torch.Tensor,
    dtype: torch.dtype = torch.bfloat16,
) -> torch.Tensor:
    """
    PyTorch reference: dequant MXFP4 + E8M0 scale -> f32 -> mm -> dtype.
    Same logic as aiter op_tests/test_gemm_a4w4.run_torch.
    x: [m, k//2] fp4 packed, w: [n, k//2] fp4 packed
    x_scales: [m, k//32] E8M0, w_scales: [n, k//32] E8M0
    Returns: [m, n] in dtype
    """
    from aiter.utility import fp4_utils

    m, _ = x.shape
    n, _ = w.shape
    # fp4 packed -> f32
    x_f32 = fp4_utils.mxfp4_to_f32(x)
    w_f32 = fp4_utils.mxfp4_to_f32(w)
    # E8M0 scale: [*, k//32] -> repeat 32 along k -> f32
    x_scales = x_scales[:m].repeat_interleave(SCALE_GROUP_SIZE, dim=1)
    x_scales_f32 = fp4_utils.e8m0_to_f32(x_scales)
    x_f32 = x_f32 * x_scales_f32
    w_scales = w_scales[:n].repeat_interleave(SCALE_GROUP_SIZE, dim=1)
    w_scales_f32 = fp4_utils.e8m0_to_f32(w_scales)
    w_f32 = w_f32 * w_scales_f32
    return torch.mm(x_f32, w_f32.T).to(dtype)[:m, :n]


def ref_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    """
    Reference: MXFP4 per-1x32 quant on A and B; both PyTorch ref and gemm_a4w4 are given.
    Returns gemm_a4w4 for check_implementation.
    """
    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
    A = A.contiguous()
    B = B.contiguous()
    m, k = A.shape
    n, _ = B.shape
    
    # 1) PyTorch impl just for your reference: dequant fp4 + e8m0 -> f32 -> mm -> bf16
    # Per-1x32 MXFP4 quant
    # A_q, A_scale = _quant_mxfp4(A, shuffle=False)
    # B_q, B_scale = _quant_mxfp4(B, shuffle=False)

    # gemm_a4w4 expects A [M,K/2], B [N,K/2] as dtypes.fp4x2; A_scale/B_scale [*,K/32] E8M0
    # quant_func returns scale as dtypes.fp8_e8m0; gemm_a4w4 accepts E8M0, no view to uint8 needed
    # slice to exact shapes [m,k_scale] / [n,k_scale] (quant may return padded scale)
    
    # k_scale = k // SCALE_GROUP_SIZE
    # A_scale = A_scale[:m, :k_scale].contiguous()
    # B_scale = B_scale[:n, :k_scale].contiguous()
    # out_torch = run_torch_fp4_mm(A_q, B_q, A_scale, B_scale, torch.bfloat16)

    # 2) aiter.gemm_a4w4 path: needs shuffled B_q and shuffled scales (see test_gemm_a4w4.py:102-105)
    A_q, A_scale_sh = _quant_mxfp4(A, shuffle=True)
    # to be noted, aiter also has other a4w4 implements using triton, https://github.com/ROCm/aiter/blob/main/aiter/ops/triton/gemm/basic/gemm_afp4wfp4.py
    out_gemm = aiter.gemm_a4w4(
        A_q,
        B_shuffle,
        A_scale_sh,
        B_scale_sh,
        dtype=dtypes.bf16,
        bpreshuffle=True,
    )
    return out_gemm

check_implementation = make_match_reference(ref_kernel, rtol=1e-02, atol=1e-02) and then try GEMM again

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user is competing in the Luma AMD Speedrun competition, optimizing GPU kernels for AMD MI355X. This session had two main phases:
   
   **Phase 1 - `/learn` and `/compact`**: Extract knowledge from prior session failures into skills.
   
   **Phase 2 - GEMM Retry**: The user provided an UPDATED reference implementation that reveals the root cause of all prior GEMM failures was NOT "callsite sensitivity" but an **unpatched quantization bug** (ROCm/aiter #974, #975). The user explicitly said "then try GEMM again" — directing me to implement a working GEMM kernel using `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` from `aiter.ops.triton.quant`.
   
   Competition details:
   - Leaderboard: `amd-mxfp4-mm`
   - GPU: AMD Instinct MI355X
   - Deadline: 2026-03-30 06:00 UTC
   - Ranking: geometric mean of benchmark results across 6 shapes
   - Top 5 advance to next round (e2e optimization for DeepSeek-R1-MXFP4 and GPTOSS-MXFP4)
   - Benchmark shapes: M∈{4,16,32,32,64,256}, N∈{2880,2112,4096,2880,7168,3072}, K∈{512,7168,512,512,2048,1536}

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **`dynamic_mxfp4_quant`** from `aiter.ops.triton.quant` — the #975-patched Triton kernel that correctly quantizes bf16 to MXFP4. This is the fix for all prior GEMM failures.
   - **`get_triton_quant` dispatches to unpatched kernel** — the old `fp4_utils.py` path produces systematically wrong quantization (ROCm/aiter #974)
   - **`e8m0_shuffle`** — shuffles E8M0 block scales to the layout expected by `gemm_a4w4` with `bpreshuffle=True`
   - **`aiter.gemm_a4w4`** (torch op) — schema: `(A, B, A_scale, B_scale, bias=None, dtype=15, alpha=1., beta=0., bpreshuffle=True)`. Does NOT accept `log2_k_split`.
   - **`aiter.gemm_a4w4_asm`** (JIT module) — lower-level: `(A, B, A_scale, B_scale, out, kernelName, bias=None, alpha=1.0, beta=0.0, bpreshuffle=True, log2_k_split=None)`. Requires pre-allocated output and kernel name.
   - **Pre-compiled HIP kernels**: Only two `.co` files exist: `f4gemm_bf16_per1x32Fp4_BpreShuffle_32x128.co` and `f4gemm_bf16_per1x32Fp4_BpreShuffle_192x128.co`
   - **CUDA/HIP graphs on MI355X**: Add overhead for short kernel sequences (~50% slower due to `copy_` overhead for static buffers)
   - **Aiter reference performance** (GEMM-only, not including quant): geomean ~11.5 µs
   - **Quantization overhead**: ~10-13 µs per call, roughly equal to GEMM time itself

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py`** (FINAL working version on leaderboard):
     - The core deliverable — uses patched `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` to bypass the unpatched `get_triton_quant` dispatch
     - Modified 5 times during this session (simple → log2_k_split → gemm_a4w4_asm → CUDA graphs → back to simple)
     ```python
     """
     MXFP4 GEMM — Direct dynamic_mxfp4_quant (bypassing unpatched get_triton_quant).
     Uses patched kernel from aiter.ops.triton.quant (ROCm/aiter #975).
     """
     from task import input_t, output_t
     from aiter import dtypes
     import aiter
     from aiter.ops.triton.quant import dynamic_mxfp4_quant
     from aiter.utility.fp4_utils import e8m0_shuffle
     
     
     def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
         x_fp4, bs_e8m0 = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(A)
         A_q = x_fp4.view(dtypes.fp4x2)
         A_scale_sh = e8m0_shuffle(bs_e8m0).view(dtypes.fp8_e8m0)
         return aiter.gemm_a4w4(
             A_q, B_shuffle, A_scale_sh, B_scale_sh,
             dtype=dtypes.bf16, bpreshuffle=True,
         )
     ```

   - **`kernels/mxfp4-mm/reference.py`** (OLD version on disk, still uses `get_triton_quant`):
     - NOT updated on disk — the runner still has this old version
     - The user provided a NEW reference separately that uses `dynamic_mxfp4_quant`
     ```python
     def ref_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
         A = A.contiguous()
         B = B.contiguous()
         quant_func = aiter.get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
         A_q, A_scale_sh = quant_func(A, shuffle=True)
         out_gemm = aiter.gemm_a4w4(
             A_q, B_shuffle, A_scale_sh, B_scale_sh,
             dtype=dtypes.bf16, bpreshuffle=True,
         )
         return out_gemm
     ```

   - **NEW reference.py** (provided by user, NOT on disk):
     - Key difference: uses `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` directly, imports `e8m0_shuffle`
     - Note in docstring: "Explicitly uses dynamic_mxfp4_quant from aiter.ops.triton.quant (patched in #975) rather than going through aiter.get_triton_quant, which may dispatch to the unpatched fp4_utils.py kernel"
     ```python
     from aiter.ops.triton.quant import dynamic_mxfp4_quant
     from aiter.utility.fp4_utils import e8m0_shuffle
     
     def _quant_mxfp4(x, shuffle=True):
         x_fp4, bs_e8m0 = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(x)
         if shuffle:
             bs_e8m0 = e8m0_shuffle(bs_e8m0)
         return x_fp4.view(dtypes.fp4x2), bs_e8m0.view(dtypes.fp8_e8m0)
     ```

   - **`~/.claude/skills/amd-triton-jit-callsite-correctness/SKILL.md`** (updated to v1.4.0):
     - Updated root cause section: "Previously misdiagnosed as 'JIT callsite sensitivity' — actually an UNPATCHED QUANTIZATION BUG"
     - Added sections: `get_torch_quant` fails, `gemm_afp4wfp4` Triton type incompatibility, dynamic module loading doesn't bypass, Popcorn single-file limitation
     - Then further updated root cause with corrected explanation about `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` being the fix

   - **`~/.claude/skills/popcorn-cli-amd-kernel-submission/SKILL.md`** (updated):
     - Added warning: "CRITICAL: Popcorn CLI only uploads `submission.py`"
     - Added `ModuleNotFoundError` symptom documentation and dynamic module workaround

   - **`~/.claude/skills/aiter-mxfp4-api-limitations/SKILL.md`** (read, not modified):
     - Contains Limitation 3 ("get_torch_quant ≠ get_triton_quant") which is now partially obsoleted by the #975 patch discovery

4. Errors and Fixes:

   - **`log2_k_split` via `aiter.gemm_a4w4`** — `RuntimeError: Unknown keyword argument 'log2_k_split' for operator 'aiter::gemm_a4w4'`
     - Root cause: `aiter.gemm_a4w4` is a `torch.ops.aiter` registered operator with a fixed schema that does NOT include `log2_k_split`
     - Schema: `aiter::gemm_a4w4(Tensor A, Tensor B, Tensor A_scale, Tensor B_scale, Tensor? bias=None, ScalarType dtype=15, float? alpha=1., float? beta=0., bool? bpreshuffle=True) -> Tensor`
     - Fix: Must call `gemm_a4w4_asm` directly to access `log2_k_split`, but that requires computing kernel name

   - **Direct `gemm_a4w4_asm` kernel name wrong** — `RuntimeError: gemm_a4w4_asm not find kernel gfx950f4gemm_bf16_per1x32Fp4_BpreShuffle_8x128`
     - Root cause: `get_padded_m(8, 2112, 7168, 32)` returns 8, but only `32x128` and `192x128` kernels exist as `.co` files
     - The `gemm_a4w4` wrapper has additional logic to map padded_m to available kernel tiles
     - Fix: Abandoned direct `gemm_a4w4_asm` approach — the kernel name selection logic is internal to the wrapper

   - **CUDA/HIP graphs slower** — Benchmark showed ~36 µs geomean vs ~23 µs without graphs
     - Root cause: `copy_()` overhead for 3 static input tensors per call exceeds kernel launch savings
     - For short kernel sequences (2-3 kernels, 8-34 µs total), graph replay machinery adds ~50% latency
     - Fix: Reverted to simple version without CUDA graphs

5. Problem Solving:

   **SOLVED — GEMM correctness**: The fundamental breakthrough was discovering that `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` from `aiter.ops.triton.quant` (patched in #975) bypasses the buggy `get_triton_quant` dispatch path. This achieves 0.0 max error on all test shapes when called directly from `submission.py`. The prior "callsite sensitivity" diagnosis was incorrect — it was an unpatched quantization kernel bug.

   **SOLVED — Leaderboard submission**: Successfully submitted to `amd-mxfp4-mm` leaderboard with ranked geomean of ~24.1 µs. Both public and secret benchmark runs passed.

   **ONGOING — Performance gap**: Our 24.1 µs vs leader's 9.7 µs (2.5x gap). The overhead is from quantization (~10-13 µs per call). Attempted optimizations (log2_k_split, direct asm, CUDA graphs) all failed. The gap likely requires a fused quant+GEMM kernel or fundamentally different approach.

   **Key diagnostic**: The aiter reference performance times (8-21 µs) appear to be GEMM-only, not including quantization. Our total time = quant_time + gemm_time ≈ 2× gemm_time.

# /learn - Online Learning System

**Extract reusable knowledge from this session into skills.** Evaluates what was learned, checks for existing skills, and creates new ones when valuable patterns are discovered.

---

## TABLE OF CONTENTS

| Phase       | Description                                            | Steps   |
| ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------ | ------- |
| **Phase 0** | Reference: triggers, quality criteria, skill structure | 0.1–0.4 |
| **Phase 1** | Evaluate: assess if knowledge is worth extracting      | 1.1     |
| **Phase 2** | Check Existing: search for related skills              | 2.1     |
| **Phase 3** | Create Skill: write the skill file                     | 3.1–3.2 |
| **Phase 4** | Quality Gates: final checklist before saving           | 4.1     |

**Quick Step Reference:**

- 0.1 Triggers, 0.2 Quality criteria, 0.3 What NOT to extract, 0.4 Skill structure
- 1.1 Self-evaluation questions
- 2.1 Search existing skills
- 3.1 Choose location, 3.2 Write SKILL.md
- 4.1 Final checklist

---

## PHASE 0: REFERENCE

### Step 0.1: Triggers

Invoke `/learn` after ANY task involving:

| Trigger                      | Example                                                          |
| ---------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Non-obvious debugging**    | Spent 10+ minutes investigating; solution wasn't in docs         |
| **Misleading errors**        | Error message pointed wrong direction; found real cause          |
| **Workarounds**              | Found limitation and creative solution                           |
| **Tool integration**         | Figured out how to use tool/API in undocumented way              |
| **Trial-and-error**          | Tried multiple approaches before finding what worked             |
| **Repeatable workflow**      | Multi-step task that will recur; worth standardizing             |
| **External service queries** | Fetched data from Jira, GitHub, Confluence, or other APIs        |
| **User-facing automation**   | Built something user will ask for again (reports, status checks) |

### Step 0.2: Quality Criteria

Skills must be:

- **Reusable**: Will help with future tasks (not just this instance)
- **Non-trivial**: Required discovery, OR is a valuable workflow pattern
- **Verified**: Solution actually worked, not theoretical

### Step 0.3: What NOT to Extract

- Single-step tasks with no workflow value
- One-off fixes unlikely to recur
- Knowledge easily found in official docs

### Step 0.4: Skill Structure

**Location:** `.claude/skills/[skill-name]/SKILL.md`

**Template:**

```markdown
---
name: descriptive-kebab-case-name
description: |
  [CRITICAL: This determines when the skill triggers. Include:]
  - What the skill does
  - Specific trigger conditions (exact error messages, symptoms)
  - When to use it (contexts, scenarios)
author: Claude Code
version: 1.0.0
---

# Skill Name

## Problem

[Clear description of the problem]

## Context / Trigger Conditions

[When to use - exact error messages, symptoms, scenarios]

## Solution

[Step-by-step solution]

## Verification

[How to verify it worked]

## Example

[Concrete example]

## References

[Links to documentation]
```

<details>
<summary>Writing Effective Descriptions</summary>

The description field is CRITICAL for skill discovery:

**Good:**

```yaml
description: |
  Fix for "ENOENT: no such file or directory" errors in npm monorepos.
  Use when: (1) npm run fails with ENOENT, (2) paths work in root but
  not in packages, (3) symlinked dependencies cause failures.
```

**Bad:**

```yaml
description: Helps with npm problems in monorepos.
```

</details>

**Guidelines:**

- **Concise** - Claude is smart; only add what it doesn't know
- **Under 500 lines** - Move large docs to `references/`
- **Examples over explanations** - Show, don't tell

---

## PHASE 1: EVALUATE

### Step 1.1: Self-Evaluation Questions

Ask yourself:

1. "What did I just learn that wasn't obvious before starting?"
2. "Would future-me benefit from having this documented?"
3. "Was the solution non-obvious from documentation alone?"
4. "Is this a multi-step workflow I'd repeat on similar tasks?"
5. "Did I query an external service the user will ask about again?"

**If NO to all → Skip extraction, nothing to learn.**

**Note:** External service queries (Jira tickets, GitHub PRs, Confluence pages) are almost always worth extracting - users frequently repeat these requests.

---

## PHASE 2: CHECK EXISTING

### Step 2.1: Search for Related Skills

Before creating, search for related skills:

```bash
ls .claude/skills/ 2>/dev/null
rg -i "keyword" .claude/skills/ 2>/dev/null
ls ~/.claude/pilot/skills/ 2>/dev/null
rg -i "keyword" ~/.claude/pilot/skills/ 2>/dev/null
```

| Found                | Action                           |
| -------------------- | -------------------------------- |
| Nothing related      | Create new skill                 |
| Same trigger and fix | Update existing (bump version)   |
| Partial overlap      | Update existing with new variant |

---

## PHASE 3: CREATE SKILL

### Step 3.1: Choose Location

**Project skills**: `.claude/skills/[skill-name]/SKILL.md`

### Step 3.2: Write SKILL.md

Use the template from Step 0.4. Ensure the description field contains specific trigger conditions.

---

## PHASE 4: QUALITY GATES

### Step 4.1: Final Checklist

Before finalizing:

- [ ] Description contains specific trigger conditions
- [ ] Solution verified to work
- [ ] Content specific enough to be actionable
- [ ] Content general enough to be reusable
- [ ] No sensitive information

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   - **`/compact`**: User triggered context compaction
   - **`/learn`**: User triggered knowledge extraction from the session, leading to updates of two skills
   - **"Continue"**: User directed continuation of competition work after the `/learn` phase
   - **"Document this context"**: User provided the full `amd-mixed-mla` competition description, benchmark shapes, and reference implementation for documentation and use
   - The overarching intent is optimizing GPU kernels for the Luma AMD Speedrun competition on MI355X, with MLA having the largest gap (46x) to the leader

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **MLA (Multi-head Latent Attention)**: DeepSeek R1's `forward_absorb` path with fused KV buffer (576 dims for K, first 512 for V), 1 KV head shared across 16 query heads
   - **aiter `mla_decode_fwd`**: ASM-based decode kernel using fp8 Q + fp8 KV, persistent mode with 32-way split-K + reduce pipeline
   - **`fav3_sage_attention_mxfp4_wrapper`**: Triton SAGE Attention v2 kernel for MXFP4 on gfx950 (BLOCK_M=256, BLOCK_N=128), takes separate Q,K,V in bshd layout, internally quantizes to MXFP4
   - **`fav3_sage_mxfp4_func`**: Lower-level API taking pre-quantized MXFP4 data with descale factors
   - **Memory-bound decode**: All benchmark shapes have qseqlen=1, making KV cache bandwidth the bottleneck. Leader at 4.3µs vs our 199µs (46x gap)
   - **MLA JIT build times**: ~224s total (metadata 31s + ASM 21s + reduce 171s), consumes significant portion of 12-min workflow timeout
   - **`dynamic_mxfp4_quant`**: Patched Triton kernel from `aiter.ops.triton.quant` (#975) that correctly quantizes MXFP4, bypassing buggy `get_triton_quant`
   - **CUDA/HIP graphs overhead**: ~50% slower for short kernel sequences due to `copy_()` overhead

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`~/.claude/skills/aiter-mxfp4-api-limitations/SKILL.md`** (v2.0.0 → v2.1.0)
     - Updated Limitation 3: Corrected root cause from "quantization mismatch" to "unpatched quantization bug (#974)"; documented `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` as the fix

   - **`kernels/mixed-mla/reference.py`** (read only)

     - Test shapes include qseqlen=1 AND qseqlen=4 with tp=4 and tp=8
     - Benchmark shapes: ALL qseqlen=1 (pure decode), bs∈{4,32,64,256}, kvseqlen∈{1024,8192}, tp∈{4,8}
     - Timeouts: test=900s, benchmark=900s, ranked=1200s
     - Ranking: geometric mean

   - **`kernels/mixed-mla/README.md`** (read only)
     - Reference latency: bs=4/kv=1k ~118µs, bs=256/kv=8k ~349µs (a8w8)
     - Accuracy tolerance: rtol=2e-02, atol=8e-03
     - Three KV cache formats provided: bf16, fp8, mxfp4
     - Optimization opportunities: MXFP4 KV (4x bandwidth savings), fused dequant+attention, MQA pattern

   - **`kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py`** (modified twice)
     - First version: ref_kernel delegation (passed test 0.0 error, benchmarked ~175µs, on leaderboard at ~199µs ranked)
     - Current version: torch-native attention using `torch.einsum` (NOT YET TESTED):
     ```python
     """
     MLA decode — torch-native attention bypassing aiter pipeline.
     """
     import torch
     from task import input_t, output_t

     SM_SCALE = 1.0 / (576 ** 0.5)

     def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data
         bs = config["batch_size"]
         nq = config["num_heads"]
         kvseqlen = config["kv_seq_len"]
         dv = config["v_head_dim"]  # 512
         kv = kv_data["bf16"]  # (total_kv, 1, 576)
         kv = kv.view(bs, kvseqlen, 576)  # (bs, kvseqlen, 576)
         scores = torch.einsum("bnh,bsh->bns", q, kv) * SM_SCALE
         weights = torch.softmax(scores, dim=-1)
         v = kv[:, :, :dv]  # (bs, kvseqlen, 512)
         out = torch.einsum("bns,bsd->bnd", weights, v)
         return out
     ```

   - **`kernels/mixed-mla/submission_introspect.py`** (created, used for remote introspection)
     - Discovered aiter.ops file structure including attention kernels
     - Key discovery: `fav3_sage_attention_mxfp4_wrapper.py` and `fav3_sage_attention_mxfp4.py`

   - **`kernels/mixed-mla/submission_probe2.py`** (created, used for API probing)

     - The line `from aiter.ops.triton.attention.fav3_sage_attention_mxfp4_wrapper import *` was inside `custom_kernel()` function
     - Fix: Replaced with `importlib.import_module()` calls instead of `import *`
     - No user feedback; self-diagnosed from remote execution error

5. Problem Solving:
   - **SOLVED — MLA baseline on leaderboard**: ref_kernel delegation achieves 0.0 max error, ranked geomean ~199µs

   - **Key architectural constraint**: MLA's fused KV buffer (576 dims for K, first 512 for V) is incompatible with all alternative attention kernels that expect separate K/V tensors with matching dimensions
   - **Bandwidth analysis**: For bs=256, kvseqlen=8192, fp8 KV = 1.2GB → 150µs at 8TB/s HBM bandwidth. MXFP4 would halve this. The 46x gap to leader requires fundamentally different approach beyond format changes.

If correctness fails (tolerance is rtol=2e-2, atol=8e-3 vs fp8 reference), the approach needs adjustment (possibly using fp8 KV instead of bf16 to match the reference more closely).

   **IMPORTANT CAVEAT**: The torch.einsum approach uses `kv.view(bs, kvseqlen, 576)` which assumes uniform kv_seq_len across the batch. The test shapes in task.yml use tp=4 (num_heads=32) and tp=8 (num_heads=16), meaning `config["num_heads"]` varies. The current code reads `nq = config["num_heads"]` which handles this correctly. However, the test shapes also include qseqlen=4 (not just qseqlen=1), and the current code doesn't handle qseqlen>1 correctly — Q shape would be `(bs*4, nq, 576)` not `(bs, nq, 576)`. This likely needs fixing before the test will pass all shapes.

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   - **`/compact`**: User triggered context compaction at session start
   - **`/learn then continue`**: User triggered knowledge extraction from session learnings, then requested continuation of the MLA kernel optimization work for the Luma AMD Speedrun competition
   - **`/learn`** (second time): User triggered a second knowledge extraction after the MLA hybrid submission was completed and placed on leaderboard
   - The overarching intent was optimizing the MLA decode attention kernel on AMD MI355X, which had the largest gap to the leader (46x → reduced to 23x)

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **MLA (Multi-head Latent Attention)**: DeepSeek R1's `forward_absorb` path with fused KV buffer (576 dims for K, first 512 for V), 1 KV head shared across 16 query heads
   - **aiter 3-stage MLA pipeline**: metadata computation → split-K ASM kernel → parallel reduce, with ~100-150 µs constant overhead for small batches
   - **torch.einsum bypass**: Using `torch.einsum("bnh,bsh->bns", q, kv)` for Q@K^T and `torch.einsum("bns,bsd->bnd", weights, v)` for weights@V, bypassing the entire aiter pipeline
   - **Hybrid routing strategy**: Route based on `bs * kvseqlen` threshold (400k tokens) — torch-native for small workloads, aiter fp8 ASM for large bandwidth-bound workloads
   - **qseqlen detection**: `total_q = q.shape[0]; qseqlen = total_q // bs` — test shapes may have qseqlen=4 while benchmark shapes are all qseqlen=1
   - **fav3_sage_mxfp4 incompatibility**: SAGE Attention Triton kernel requires separate K/V with same head_dim; MLA's fused buffer (K=576, V=512) is fundamentally incompatible
   - **Popcorn CLI remote submission**: Test → Benchmark → Leaderboard workflow on MI355X via GitHub Actions runners
   - **aiter JIT build overhead**: ~224s total (metadata 31s + ASM 21s + reduce 172s), happens even when custom_kernel doesn't use aiter (because eval harness imports reference.py)

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py`** (modified 3 times, final version is hybrid)
     - This is the main submission file for the MLA kernel optimization
     - Version 1: torch-native only (no qseqlen guard) — existed from prior session
     - Version 2: torch-native with qseqlen>1 fallback to ref_kernel
     - Version 3 (current): hybrid routing with 400k token threshold
     ```python
     """
     MLA decode — hybrid: torch-native for small workloads, aiter for large.
     Geomean: ~97 µs vs ~199 µs baseline (2x improvement over ref_kernel).
     """
     import torch
     from reference import ref_kernel
     from task import input_t, output_t

     SM_SCALE = 1.0 / (576 ** 0.5)
     TORCH_NATIVE_THRESHOLD = 400_000  # tokens (bs * kvseqlen)

     def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data
         bs = config["batch_size"]
         nq = config["num_heads"]
         kvseqlen = config["kv_seq_len"]
         dv = config["v_head_dim"]  # 512
         total_q = q.shape[0]
         qseqlen = total_q // bs
         if qseqlen != 1 or bs * kvseqlen > TORCH_NATIVE_THRESHOLD:
             return ref_kernel(data)
         kv = kv_data["bf16"]  # (total_kv, 1, 576)
         kv = kv.view(bs, kvseqlen, 576)
         scores = torch.einsum("bnh,bsh->bns", q, kv) * SM_SCALE
         weights = torch.softmax(scores, dim=-1)
         v = kv[:, :, :dv]
         out = torch.einsum("bns,bsd->bnd", weights, v)
         return out
     ```

   - **`kernels/mixed-mla/submission_hybrid.py`** (created as intermediate file)
     - Same content as the final submission.py, was used to benchmark the hybrid approach before promoting it to submission.py

     - Documents that fav3 requires separate K/V tensors with same head_dim (MLA has K=576, V=512)
     - Documents qseqlen>1 edge case in task.yml with safe fallback pattern

   - **`~/.claude/skills/competitive-kernel-optimization-ceiling/SKILL.md`** (updated v1.2.0 → v1.3.0)
